As implemented in `agentic-daemon` (#188, `apps/daemon/src/fs.ts`): every path is checked lexically first (answered `outside-roots` before the disk is touched), then again after `realpath` of the path and of every root, and all reads go through the resolved path; `session.open` runs the same check on its `cwd` and refuses a missing one. Git badges are read from `.git` / `HEAD` / `config` files, never by running git — `origin` from the common config (`<gitdir>/config`, or for a worktree the config its `commondir` points to). A `locate` walks each root breadth-first at most `min(depth, FS_LOCATE_MAX_DEPTH = 3)` levels down, skipping `node_modules` and dot-folders, following a symlink or junction only when it resolves to a folder inside the roots and never visiting a resolved folder twice, and stops at `FS_LOCATE_MAX_MATCHES = 20` with `truncated: true`; a root missing on disk holds nothing. `worktree` runs `git -C <repo> worktree add -b <branch> -- <path> [base]` through `execFile` with no shell and a 60 s timeout, after `git check-ref-format --branch`.

**Idempotent worktrees and project commands** (#617, contract; tracking #616). A `worktree` answer may carry `reused: true` (the worktree was already at `path` on `branch`, nothing changed) or `recreated: true` (`branch` existed with no worktree and was checked out at `path` again); a `path` holding anything else is `worktree-mismatch`. The `fs.request` kind `run { cwd, argv, timeoutMs? }` (daemon feature `run`) runs a project-configured command — `argv[0]` with its arguments, never a shell string, at most `FS_RUN_MAX_ARGS` entries, `timeoutMs` default `FS_RUN_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MS` and capped at `FS_RUN_MAX_TIMEOUT_MS` — in a `cwd` within the roots, and answers `FsRunResult { exitCode, stdoutTail, stderrTail }` (tails of at most `FS_RUN_OUTPUT_TAIL` characters; a non-zero exit is still a result, a program that cannot start `not-found`, one that runs out its time `timeout`). The Machine admits `run` from the owner only, like `worktree` and `locate`, and refuses at once, with no frame sent, a daemon without the `run` feature (`unsupported`) or a `cwd` outside the environment's `cwdRoots` (`outside-roots`). Until the daemon implements it (#618) `agentic-daemon` answers `run` with `unsupported`.

**Session files and changes** (target — tracking #568; decisions 2026-09-23; design `docs/design/HANDOFF.md` § "Session files and changes"): two read-only views beside a session's transcript, Changes (the diff of the session's folder) and Files (a browser of it). They answer "what did the agent do to my code" without leaving the platform. Files never leave the machine except what the user opens, and there are no write operations: edits stay with the agent, behind its approval rules. Every layer is a seam, so nothing below is git- or Monaco-specific by construction:
- **Wire** (#559, contract). `fs.request` gains three kinds, gated by the daemon feature `files`. Each carries `root`, the session's cwd, with `path` relative to it:
  - `tree` returns one level of files and folders, with size and change status. `.git` and `.gitignore`d entries are left out, with `FS_LIST_MAX_ENTRIES` and `truncated`.
  - `read` takes `rev: working | head | base`. It returns text up to `FS_READ_MAX_BYTES`, sized so an answer fits one frame; `too-large` over that; metadata only for a binary.
  - `changes` takes `scope: uncommitted | branch` and returns a VCS-neutral `ChangeSet`: `vcs`, `branch`, `base`, `ahead` / `behind`, files with `modified | added | deleted | renamed | untracked` and `+/-` counts, and the commits since the base.
  - A diff is two `read`s (base or head, and working). The renderer computes the hunks, so no unified-diff text crosses the wire.
  - Core's `WorkspaceSource` (`tree` / `read` / `changes`, answered like `ProjectFeatureFs`) is the one interface the pages and MCP consume. `workspaceCapabilities` decides the tabs: a runtime with no filesystem (`anthropic-api`) shows neither, and a folder with no VCS hides Changes.
  - A "Send to chat" code reference is a `resource` part with the new optional `text` (the hunk), uri `agentic-session://<sessionId>/<path>#L<n>`.
  - As contracted (#559): `FsOp` kinds `tree {root, path}`, `read {root, path, rev?, base?}`, `changes {root, scope, base?}`; results `FsTreeResult` (`FsTreeEntry { name, path, type: file | dir | symlink, size?, change? }`, `truncated`, `ignoredHidden`), `FsReadResult` (`size`, `text?` | `binary: true`, `lines?`), `ChangeSet` (`ChangedFile`, `ChangeCommit`, `FileChangeStatus`, `ChangeScope`, `FsReadRev`); the new error `too-large`. `FS_READ_MAX_BYTES` is 480 KiB, not the handoff's 1 MB: half a frame, because JSON may escape a character in two, and `isBinaryText` sends a file holding any control character JSON escapes six-fold (anything in C0 but tab, LF, CR, FF, BS) as binary — so every answer fits `LIMITS.frameBytes` with no chunking. `CHANGES_MAX_FILES` 500, `CHANGES_MAX_COMMITS` 100, `RESOURCE_TEXT_MAX_CHARS` 64 Ki. `WorkspaceSource` answers `WorkspaceAnswer<R>`; `workspaceCapabilities({ folder, features, vcs })` gives `{ files, vcs? }`; `sessionFileUri` / `parseSessionFileUri` spell the reference; `FileTouch { path, line? }` is what a runtime's touched-file extractor returns. `daemonConformance` has `files-tree`, `files-read` and `files-changes` (feature `files`, over the harness's `ConformanceFiles`), and `inMemoryHarness()` answers all three from `InMemoryFolder`s (default `IN_MEMORY_SESSION_FOLDERS`: a repository at `/work/project`, a plain folder at `/work/plain`).
- **Daemon** (#561). The daemon checks `path ⊂ root ⊂ cwdRoots`, lexically and again after realpath (`checkWithinRoots`, as for the picker). Because the check is here, a session-scoped read needs no Session→Machine hop inside a turn. As implemented in `agentic-daemon` (`apps/daemon/src/files.ts`; the root checks it shares with the picker are in `src/roots.ts`):
  - **Confinement.** A `path` that is absolute, has a drive letter or climbs out of `root` is `outside-roots` before the disk is touched; so is a link that resolves out of the session folder (not just out of the roots). Everything on disk is read through the resolved path. `hello.features` always lists `files`.
  - **tree** lists one level, folders first, then files, each case-insensitively; `.git` is never listed. With a VCS the entries it ignores are left out (`ignoredHidden: true`) and each entry carries its change status — a folder `modified` when anything under it changed. Without a VCS, or when git cannot run, everything is listed with no marks.
  - **read** of the working copy sniffs the first 8000 bytes for a NUL. A binary file — also bytes that are not UTF-8, or text `isBinaryText` rejects — answers metadata only, at any size; a text file over `FS_READ_MAX_BYTES` is `too-large`. `head` / `base` read the committed blob (`cat-file --batch-check`, then `cat-file blob`), so a deleted file still reads at `head`; a file not in that revision is `not-found`; a folder under no VCS is `not-a-repo`.
  - **`VcsProvider`** (`src/vcs/provider.ts`): `open(folder)` returns a `VcsRepo { changes, status, ignored, show }` or `undefined`; the first provider that owns the folder answers, and `FsOptions.vcs` passes others. Git (`src/vcs/git.ts`) runs `git -C <folder> -c core.quotepath=off …` through `runGit` (`src/vcs/run.ts`: `spawn` with no shell, a timeout, `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`, stdout capped) and reads machine formats only: `rev-parse --show-prefix` (a session folder below the repo root sees paths relative to itself), `status --porcelain=v2 --branch -z --untracked-files=all`, `check-ignore --stdin -z`, `diff --numstat -z -M --relative` and `--name-status`, `rev-list --left-right --count`, `log -z`. `uncommitted` is the working tree against HEAD (index included; the empty tree before the first commit; untracked files counted from disk); `branch` is HEAD against its merge-base with the base. Both carry the branch's commits since the merge-base, newest first. Without git, `changes` and committed reads are `unsupported`.
- **The base.** It is the project git feature's `base` setting (an explicit `base` that does not resolve is `not-found`), otherwise `main`, otherwise `master`, otherwise what `origin/HEAD` names. The upstream is not used: a pushed feature branch's upstream is the branch itself. With no base, or no merge-base, there is no `base` / `ahead` / `behind`, no commits, and the `branch` scope is empty.
- **Machine** (#562, `packages/platform/src/machine`). `fsRequest(environmentId, op)` admits `tree` / `read` / `changes` for session drivers (`sessionDriver`, like `list`), in the picker's order: 400 malformed, 403 revoked, 404 unknown environment, 503 offline. Then it refuses at once, with no frame sent, what it can judge itself: a daemon whose `hello.features` lacks `files` → `unsupported`, a `root` not lexically inside the environment's `cwdRoots` (core `pathWithin`) → `outside-roots`. The answers go to an activation-local map, never the saved record (a `read` weighs up to half a frame), as history's do. The record keeps only the status. The `fsAnswer(requestId)` stream yields `FsAnswer = WorkspaceAnswer<FsResult>` once the answer lands or the request fails: `timeout` at `fsTimeoutMs` or on a disconnect, the refusal, or the daemon's own error. `fsResult` shows the held result too. An answer that landed on another activation reads `internal` (ask again). The last `changes` answer per `(environmentId, root, scope)` is saved with its time: `MAX_CHANGES_SNAPSHOTS` = 8, the least recently answered evicted, each cut to `SNAPSHOT_MAX_FILES` = 200 files and `SNAPSHOT_MAX_COMMITS` = 50 commits with `truncated`. It is read with `changesSnapshot(environmentId, root, scope)` → `{ at, result } | null` and survives disconnects, so an offline machine still shows a dated snapshot. **The one client** is `machineWorkspaceSource(client, environmentId, root, { base? })` (`machine/files.ts`): a `WorkspaceSource` over any Machine actor client the caller builds with its own principal. Each call is `fsRequest`, then the first `fsAnswer`. `base` rides `changes` and `read(…, 'base')`. The Machine's `ServerFnError`s (403 / 404 / 503) throw; everything else is the answer's `error`. The web pages (#564) and the MCP port (#566) use it.
- **ui** (#563). `CodeRenderer { Viewer, Diff }` is provided through `provideCodeRenderer`:
  - The default is Monaco (`@sigx/monaco-editor`, whose diff editor, line-click, view-zone and language-by-path helpers were added upstream, #560), lazy-loaded. Its theme is generated from the design tokens.
  - A plain line-grid renderer serves SSR, no-JS and tests.
  - The generic parts are `FileTree`, `ChangeList`, `CommitList`, `SessionBar`, `LineComposer` and `FileHeader`.
- **Web** (#564, #565). `/sessions/:id/changes?file=&scope=&view=unified|split` and `/sessions/:id/files?path=` share the session bar with the transcript.
  - As implemented (#564, `apps/web/src/pages/session/`): every view renders from a `SessionFiles` (`files.ts`) — the `WorkspaceSource`, whether the folder has files and a VCS, the machine's name and presence, its offline snapshot, the chat, and the three seams #565 fills (`ask`, `mention`, `fileActions`; absent, their controls are not drawn). `files-sources.ts` builds it: in mock mode over `memoryWorkspaceSource` (`mock/files.ts`, a folder model with texts per revision, change sets computed), live from the Session's `spec` (`machineId`, `environmentId`, `cwd`) and the Machine's record through `machineWorkspaceSource`, the base being the chat's project git feature `base`. `workspaceCapabilities` over the machine's `hello.features` decides Files; Changes shows until a `changes` answer is `not-a-repo`.
  - `useSessionChanges` loads one `ChangeSet`; offline (or on a 503) it shows the Machine's `changesSnapshot` with its time under a "Machine disconnected" banner, refresh disabled. `not-found` on the folder is "Folder no longer on <machine>". With nothing uncommitted and no `scope` in the query, Changes says so and shows the branch. A diff is two `read`s (head → working, or base → head); `too-large` links to Files, a binary shows its size.
  - The web client's Machine ref declares its streams (`fsAnswer`, `historyAnswer`), so `machineWorkspaceSource` over `actor(Machine, key)` works on the wire. The prebundled Monaco is served at `/monaco-bundle` (`monacoPrebundledPlugin` in `apps/web/vite.config.ts`; the build emits it into the Worker's static assets). Below 768 px the list (or tree) and the file are two screens, the file with a back link; the layout switch is hidden there.
  - "Ask about a line" posts to the session's chat, addressed to its agent, with the hunk attached.
  - "Mention in chat" inserts an `@file:` reference token.
  - "Edited by … at" and "View diff" on tool cards come from a per-runtime touched-files registry in `@agentic/runtimes`; claude-code registers Edit, Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit.
  - As implemented (#565, `apps/web/src/pages/session/chat-hooks.tsx`, `references.ts`): `ask` is `askInChat` — it reads the chat's summary and last `CONTEXT_WINDOW` entries, refuses an agent that is no longer a member (never re-addressed to the coordinator), and runs the chat's own `runActivation` with the question as text, a `resource` part `{ uri: sessionFileUri(sessionId, path, line), mediaType: 'text/x-diff', text: <hunk, ≤ RESOURCE_TEXT_MAX_CHARS> }` and `mentions: [the session's agent]`. The task's objective carries each snippet after the question as a labelled fenced block (`resourceText`), and the thread renders such a part the same way; a `resource` part without text stays `[uri]`. `mention` opens `/chats/<chat>?file=<sessionFileUri>`: the chat page (live and mock) inserts `@file:<path> ` through the Composer's generic `insert` prop once, drops the query, and a message holding the token carries a `resource` part for that file; `mentionsIn` never reads `@file:` as a member. Tool paths are absolute, so both views read `?file=` / `?path=` relative to the session's folder (`relativeToRoot`). `filesTouched(runtime, call)` (`registerFileTouches` for more runtimes; a runtime without an extractor shows neither link) feeds `ToolCall`'s generic `links` slot (`Thread.toolLinks`) with "View diff" for a call a chat feed's session made, and `fileActions` with the last call that wrote each file — live from the Session's `events` (linking to the Transcript; events carry no time), mock from the chat's tool calls (with the message time). The mock workspace keeps posted questions for the visit (`mock/chat-posts.ts`) and shows them after the chat's rows.
- **Worktrees** (#622, tracking #616). The `fs.request` kind `worktrees { root }` (daemon feature `worktrees`) answers `FsWorktreesResult`: the worktrees of the repository `root` is in, as `git worktree list --porcelain -z` has them (at most `FS_WORKTREES_MAX`), each `FsWorktreeEntry { path, branch?, head?, detached?, locked?, prunable?, current?, outside? }` — `current` the one `root` is in, `outside` one beyond the environment's `cwdRoots` (as written or resolved). The Machine treats it as a session-files kind: open to session drivers, refused at once without the feature (`unsupported`) or for a `root` outside the roots, the answer held by the activation and read with `fsAnswer` (`machineWorktrees(client, environmentId, root)` beside `machineWorkspaceSource`). On the web, `SessionFiles.worktrees` lists them (live only when the machine's daemon has the feature; the mock repo has three) and `SessionFiles.at(root)` is the same views over another worktree's folder — the chat hooks left out, `sessionRoot` naming the session's own. The session bar on Changes and Files shows a picker (two or more worktrees): the session's own marked "this session", one outside the roots or whose folder is gone disabled; picking one puts `?root=` on the view (the frame applies it with `filesAtRoot`, the tabs and links keep it with `rootQuery`). The session itself never moves (EXE-12).
- **MCP** (#566). `sessions_files_list(sessionId, path?)`, `sessions_files_read(sessionId, path, rev?)` and `sessions_changes(sessionId, scope?)` are read-only tools under the `sessions` scope, over `PlatformPort.sessions.tree` / `read` / `changes`. The app's port (`apps/web/src/auth/oauth-server/port.ts`) reads the Session under the client's principal — the Session's `sessions` policy decides whether the client may see it — then builds a `machineWorkspaceSource` rooted at `spec.cwd` on `spec.environmentId`. The Machine is asked as the workspace driver, as `usage_limits` does, because the Machine admits only `machines` readers and a `sessions` client must not also need that scope. The client still cannot reach past the session: every request is rooted at its folder and the daemon confines paths to it. `base` is the project git feature's `base` when the session's task is in a project; otherwise the daemon picks. A session with no folder on a machine (an API runtime) is a 400 before any machine is asked. A refusal (`outside-roots`, `not-found`, `not-a-repo`, `too-large`, `unsupported`, …) is an error result `<tool>: <code>: <message>`. `sessions_files_read` answers like `chats_file_get`: a JSON summary block `{ sessionId, path, rev, uri, kind, size, lines?, note? }` (the `uri` is `sessionFileUri`), then the text for a text file; a binary file is the summary alone (`kind: 'metadata'`).

**The `agentic-daemon` command** (#354, `apps/daemon/src/launcher.ts`): the daemon ships as an unpacked folder started by a service, so nothing puts its CLI on `PATH` — yet every instruction the product gives (Machine page, Pair page, both READMEs, the runbook) is spelled `agentic-daemon …`. The install scripts call `launcher install`, which writes a launcher that hard-codes the Node and entry the install resolved (so it runs where no Node is on `PATH`) and then takes the least invasive route onto `PATH`: its own folder already there → nothing, `~/.local/bin` or `~/bin` on `PATH` → a symlink, otherwise a fenced block in the profile `$SHELL` names, or the Windows **user** `PATH` via `[Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable`. `launcherPlan` decides all of it from injected `platform` / `env` / `home` and touches no disk, so every OS's decision is tested from any one of them; `launcher remove` (the uninstall scripts) undoes each route. The web end is the Machine page's "command not found?" disclosure (`pages/machines/manage.ts` `fallbackCommand`), which spells the same command as `node <install folder>/bin/agentic-daemon.mjs …` for a machine installed before this.

**`agentic-daemon open`** (#336, `apps/daemon/src/open.ts`): the command-line entry into a chat, and no daemon frame — the daemon resolves the folder locally and the web resolves everything else from the query. `open [path] [--env <id>] [--no-browser]` takes the folder (default the current directory) and, over the daemon's own `environments.json`, finds the environment whose `cwdRoots` contain it with `checkWithinRoots` (lexically, then with links resolved, so the answer is the one `fs.request` would give); no environment → exit 1 with every environment's roots listed; several → exit 2 naming them until `--env <id>` picks one (checked the same way, exit 1 when the folder is not under its roots). It reads the folder's `gitInfo` for the `origin` (absent when the folder is not a checkout), prints `<platform>/chats/new?env=<id>&path=<lexical native path>[&origin=<url>]` (each value `encodeURIComponent`-ed; the platform URL from `credentials.json`, normalized as `pair` normalizes it) and opens it with the OS opener (`cmd /c start "" "<url>"`, `open`, `xdg-open`) unless `--no-browser`; the link is printed before the opener runs, so a headless box still gets it, and an opener that fails is a note, not a failure. Unpaired, or an unloadable `environments.json`, is exit 1 like `run`. The web end is §10 "Entry points".

**Web-managed environments** (#224, frames #236; decisions 2026-09-19 (c)): `env.request` asks a daemon to create or change (`put`, an upsert) or to `remove` an environment. `EnvironmentInput` is `{ id?, name, runtime, cwdRoots, concurrency?, accountLabel? }`.
- It has **no `profileDir`**. The daemon allocates one per environment, and its schema is strict: a frame whose input carries any key the contract does not name fails validation instead of having the key stripped.
- `env.response` answers with `{ environmentId }` or a named refusal: `policy-disabled`, `outside-allowed-roots`, `unknown-runtime`, `in-use`, `unknown-environment`, `invalid`, `io` (and `timeout`, which only the platform sets).
- The descriptors themselves travel in an `env` frame that comes with a `result`, before or after it.
- `hello` and `env` carry the optional `policy: { webManaged, allowedRoots, source?, requested?, locked? }`, the machine's policy as it stands. Since #355 the web sets it through `policy.request` (below); a daemon that predates it sends none, and one whose owner locked it reports `locked` and refuses every request.
- `daemonConformance` has `env-put`, `env-remove` and `env-policy` for daemons whose harness declares the `env-manage` feature. `agentic-daemon` does not declare it until it answers the frame (#238); the Machine actor's end is below.

The Machine actor is the platform end (#189). The daemon's reply arrives as a separate `socketMessage` turn on the same actor, so the answer is a **stored result**, never an awaited promise:
- `fsRequest(environmentId, op)` → `{ requestId }` (`fs_<uuid>`) validates the op against the protocol schema (400), refuses a revoked machine (403), an unknown environment (404) and an offline machine or one with no open socket (503), sends `fs.request` and stores `fs[requestId] = { environmentId, op, status: 'pending', requestedAt, deadline, by }`. `list` is open to session drivers (a user, an agent, an external client with `sessions`); `worktree` changes the machine and `locate` walks every root of it (#332), so only its owner (a user principal) may ask for those — checked inside the method, since `methodAuthorize` sees only the method. The router's driver is the workspace user, so a project feature plugin's `fs` (§7) may ask for both.
- `fsResult(requestId)` → `FsResultView { requestId, environmentId, op, status: pending | done | error, requestedAt, finishedAt?, result?, error? }`, 404 for an unknown, evicted or pruned id; one string argument, so the UI reads it live with `useActorState(defs.Machine, () => [k, 'fsResult', id], { live: true })`. Same principals as `fsRequest`.
- The `fs.response` case stores the result or error. An unknown id is ignored, and so is a second answer, except over a `timeout`: a late answer says what the daemon did. A result of the wrong kind is stored as `internal`.
- Bounds: a pending request fails `{ code: 'timeout' }` at its deadline (`fsTimeoutMs`, 30 s) through the `liveness` reminder, which stays armed while any request is pending; `socketClosed` fails every pending one with `timeout` "machine went offline" (`revoke` with "machine revoked"). At most `MAX_FS_REQUESTS` (16) entries are kept, the oldest evicted first, and finished ones are pruned `FS_RESULT_TTL_MS` (2 min) after they finished (on the next `fsRequest` or reminder tick).
- A successful `worktree` records `workdir.worktree-created` (§4 Audit) under the principal that asked, keyed `{machineKey}:worktree:{requestId}`.

Environment requests use the same machinery (#237):
- `putEnvironment(input: EnvironmentInput)` / `removeEnvironment(environmentId)` → `{ requestId }` (`env_<uuid>`) send `env.request` and store `envRequests[requestId] = { op, status: 'pending', requestedAt, deadline, by }`; `envResult(requestId)` → `EnvResultView { requestId, op, status, requestedAt, finishedAt?, result?, error? }`, read live like `fsResult`. The input is parsed by the strict `environmentInput` schema (400, so a `profileDir` never leaves the platform); 403 revoked; 503 `machine-offline` with no daemon or no open socket. `removeEnvironment` also refuses an environment the machine does not report (404) and one with a turn running or a session queued in it (409 `in-use`, naming them — #394); idle hosted sessions are closed first (`session.close`), and the daemon checks its own side too.
- **Owner only, and on no tool surface.** An agent must not widen where agents may work (decisions 2026-09-19 (c)): `methodAuthorize` admits a user principal only, and a source scan (`packages/platform/__tests__/machine/tool-boundary.test.ts`) fails if the platform tools, the daemon `tool.call` port or the platform MCP server ever call the methods — the policy, restart, log and login methods of #355 included.
- **The policy itself is set from the web (#355, #480; decisions 2026-09-22 — supersedes "edited only on the machine").** The Machine keeps the **desired** folders (`policyDesired`), set by `setPolicy` (owner, elevated — `requireElevated`) or preset on the Pair page (`registerMachinePending({ allowedRoots })` → `claimPairing` → `pair`), and sends them as `policy.request { op: 'set' }`; the daemon expands `~` to its user's home, checks every folder as `allow-root` does, writes `policy.json` with `source: 'web'` and `requested` (the roots as asked), and answers `policy.response` (read with `policyResult`) after the `env` frame carrying the policy. Convergence is core's `policyConverged(desired, reported, os)`: `source: 'web'` and the same requested strings, `~` literal — no home directory reaches the platform. **Reconcile** (`shouldReconcile`, `machine/policy.ts`): on `hello` and `env` only, once per drift, never while a request is in flight, never to a `locked` machine, and after a refused automatic request only when the owner changes the set or the daemon reconnects — one request per connect plus the local drifts, no loop. `browseMachine(path?)` (owner, elevated) lists the machine's folders for the picker. `allowBypassPermissions: true` on `putEnvironment` for an environment that lacks it is elevated; `revoke` is elevated; `rename` is audited (`machine.renamed`); the first elevated change of an elevation window records `auth.elevated`. `agentic-daemon policy lock` on the machine refuses every request `policy-locked` and the page shows the unlock command.
- The daemon decides. Its refusal (`policy-disabled`, `outside-allowed-roots`, `unknown-runtime`, `in-use`, `invalid`, `io`, `unknown-environment`) is stored unchanged; the descriptors arrive with the daemon's `env` frame. Timeout, disconnect, revoke, the late-answer rule and the bounds (`envTimeoutMs` 30 s, `MAX_ENV_REQUESTS` 16, `ENV_RESULT_TTL_MS` 2 min) are the folder requests'.
- Each answered request is recorded once — `environment.put` / `environment.removed`, keyed `{machineKey}:env:{requestId}` — with the roots that were asked for and the outcome (`ok` or the daemon's code). An unanswered one is not: whether the daemon applied it is unknown until it answers.
- `MachineView.policy` is the `MachinePolicy` from the last `hello` (dropped when a daemon sends none) and any `env` that carries one, so the Machine page can explain "web management is off on this machine" and offer the local command.
- **Sign-in from the web (#484):** `requestLogin(environmentId)` (owner, not elevated — signing in narrows nothing; 404 an environment the machine lacks, 409 a daemon without the `login` feature or a runtime whose `CapabilityReport.login` is not `relay`, or a sign-in already running for it, 503 offline) sends `login.request` and keeps `logins[environmentId] = { requestId, phase, startedAt, deadline, by }`; the daemon's `login.status` frames move `phase` and set `action` / `error`; `answerLogin(environmentId, text)` forwards the pasted text as `login.answer` inside the call and stores it nowhere (400 empty or over `LOGIN_ANSWER_MAX_CHARS`, 409 when nothing is expected); `cancelLogin` sends `login.cancel` (offline, it ends the record `cancelled` here); `loginState(environmentId)` is the live read. A disconnect fails a running one `failed`, a revoke `cancelled`, the liveness reminder `timeout` past `LOGIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (eleven minutes, past the daemon's own cap); an ended one is kept `LOGIN_RESULT_TTL_MS`. Every end is audited `machine.login { environmentId, outcome }`. The daemon (`apps/daemon/src/login-relay.ts`) runs the runtime's own CLI with piped stdio and parses it — a cancel or timeout ends the CLI's whole process tree (`taskkill /T` on Windows, where it runs under `cmd.exe`; #520); the page (`LoginDialog`) shows the link or the device code.

History requests (#397) use the same machinery, with one difference — the answer is not stored on the record:
- `historyRequest(sessionId, range: HistoryRange)` → `{ requestId }` (`history_<uuid>`; session drivers, like `sendCommand`) sends `history.request` and stores `history[requestId] = { sessionId, range, status: 'pending', requestedAt, deadline }`; 403 revoked, 503 `machine-offline` with no daemon or no open socket. The session need not be hosted here any more: the log outlives the runtime session.
- The `history.response` case turns the record `done` — the events (unwrapped from their `event` frames) go to an activation-local map, never to the record, since one answer weighs up to half a frame and the Machine record is saved whole — or `error` with the daemon's code stored unchanged (`gap`, `unknown-session`, `internal`).
- The stream `historyAnswer(requestId)` yields once: the events (with `more`), or the error — when the response has landed, or the request failed (`historyTimeoutMs` 30 s through the `liveness` reminder, `socketClosed`, `revoke`, all `internal`); an id the record does not hold, or an answer the activation no longer has, is `internal` too and the caller asks again. `historyResult(requestId)` is the status view, `MAX_HISTORY_REQUESTS` 16 / `HISTORY_RESULT_TTL_MS` 60 s the bounds.
- `machineHistorySource(() => Machine)` (`packages/platform/src/machine/history.ts`) is the Session's `HistorySource` over the two: a fresh `actor()` call as the workspace user (the Session's own turn is where it runs; the answer arrives on a Machine turn of its own), `historyRequest` then `for await` on `historyAnswer`. `apps/web` wires it as `defineSessionActor({ history })`; without the port a Session forgets nothing.

- Machine DO routes `session.frame` → `actor(Session, id).forwardFrames(frames)` (one-way; `hello` records capabilities and lifts `disconnected` — not the ref it carries, which is the open's placeholder; `event` folds, `gap` marks `disconnected` and moves the head); `session.ref` → `Session.noteRef(ref)` after updating the hosted session's own `ref` (#389: the runtime's id, appended only when its identity — `id`, `data.epoch` — changed; a session the machine does not host is ignored); `session.title` → `Session.noteTitle(title)` (#460: nothing is kept on the record — it publishes a `title` session event to its chat; a session the machine does not host is ignored, and a record's refusal is swallowed like a frame's); `session.reply` → `Session.commandReplied` (a prompt ack marks the turn running, its `turn-end` frame snapshots the transcript and posts to the chat); `tool.call` → the platform tool under an **agent principal** `{kind: 'agent', ws, agentId, sessionId, taskId}` so Memory/Task authorize correctly. The Machine mints that principal from the task the session was OPENED for (`HostedSession.taskId`, fixed at `openSession`) — identity only: the turn owns the task (#390, §5a), so `createToolCallPort` (`routing/tool-call.ts`) reads the Session once per call and rebuilds the principal with `running.taskId ?? spec.taskId` before the tool runs; the same read gives `chat_post` / `ask_user` their chat and the memory tools their gate. One Session hop per daemon tool call, on a path that is already detached from the socket's turn. A Session's refusal of a `session.frame` or a `session.reply` (403 from `assertHostingMachine`: a session this machine does not host on the record — stale after a daemon restart, re-opened elsewhere, never opened here) never reaches the Machine (#393, #492): every hand-off to the Session — `forwardFrames`, `noteRef`, `noteTitle`, `commandReplied` — is dispatched one-way, so the Machine's turn ends at the Session's acceptance and a refusal, or any other failure of the record's turn, is dropped where it lands (dev: a host warning); what the dispatch itself throws — a failure before acceptance, an authorization or placement bug — still surfaces. The frame is dropped and the socket stays up, since the host's `webSocketMessage` would otherwise take every other session on it down with the throw — and the Machine is never held by a Session's slow turn-end (§3 "Hub turns"): a session's slow turn used to queue every other session's frames and every placement read (`get`, `openSession`) behind it, up to the 30 s deadline.
- **`chat_post` mentions over the bridge** (#599 Finding 1): the daemon's bridged `chat_post` sends the harness's arguments as they are, and `createToolCallPort` re-runs the same definition, so `mentions` reaches `activateMentions` unchanged (`mentions.test.ts` drives it `tool.call` → `tool.result`). The drop seen in a real session was upstream of the bridge: the model wrote its mentions INTO `text` as its own call markup (`…</text>\n<parameter name="mentions">["agent_…"]`), so the call carried no `mentions` key. `chatPostTool` recovers that shape (`recoverLeakedParameters`, `packages/runtimes/src/tools/chat.ts`) when the call names neither `mentions` nor `attachments` and everything after `</text>` is `mentions` / `attachments` blocks holding JSON string arrays: the markup is cut from the text and the arrays become the parameters; anything else is left as the agent's text. And every id in a non-empty `mentions` comes back in `activated` or `notActivated` — the poster's own id too (`the poster itself: …`), so a post never answers `{messageId}` alone for a mention.
- Session → daemon: `Session.prompt` records the command as pending (idempotent while the reply is out) and hands it to the `CommandSink` port — the app binds it to `actor(Machine).sendCommand(sessionId, cmd)` → socket. Pending replies are stored in Machine state with a deadline so evictions do not leak promises.
- Reconnect: the daemon redials with backoff; `welcome.wanted` cursors drive gapless replay from the daemon's per-session NDJSON log (`%LOCALAPPDATA%/agentic/sessions/{id}.ndjson`); beyond that a `gap` marks the Session `disconnected` with "events lost" (OPS-04, distinct from failure). After a daemon restart a wanted session is replayed from that log and closed with `code: 'restart'`; the platform re-opens it with `spec.resume`, on the same log and a head that continues (#363).
- **Driver seam** (`@agentic/core` `RuntimeDriver<S, P>`): the daemon reads `LocalEnvironment` rows from `environments.json`, asks the driver to `inspect(env)` (auth status, isolation, `CapabilityReport`) and runs its `doctor(envs)` for `hello` / `env` via `toEnvironmentDescriptor`; `hello.capabilities` carries one report per runtime — an environment's `inspect` report, else the driver's environment-free `report()` (#541), so a runtime can be offered for its first environment; a driver without `report` (a harness that is not installed) is not reported (the per-environment verdict `EnvironmentDescriptor.doctor = environmentVerdict(report, id, checkedAt)`; a throwing driver gives each of its environments an error verdict `driver-doctor-failed`), `open(env, OpenSpec, { sessionId, callTool, policy })` → `{ session, capabilities, title? }` on `session.open` (`title()` — the runtime's current title for the conversation, when the runtime keeps one (#460): the Claude Code driver reads the newest `custom-title` / `ai-title` row from the tail of `<configDir>/projects/<slug(cwd)>/<sessionId>.jsonl` (`readSessionTitle`), the Copilot driver keeps the CLI's `session.title_changed`; Codex has `thread/name/updated` but the driver does not read it yet), and runs `doctor(envs)` for `agentic-daemon doctor`. `callTool` is how a driver's platform tools reach the socket as `tool.call`. The daemon never branches on the runtime id; it picks the driver whose `runtime` matches the row.
- Multi-account isolation = `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` per environment (the adapter's env allowlist passes it through). `claudeCodeDriver` (`@agentic/runtimes/claude-code`, #20) sets it from `profileDir` and removes the daemon's own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `ANTHROPIC_*` from every child, so an environment can only ever use its own account; `inspect` reads auth status from `<config dir>/.credentials.json`; every session runs with Claude Code's cross-session tools (`ListAgents`, `SendMessage` — they reach the operator's other Claude Code sessions, not platform agents, and run without a permission request) in the SDK's `disallowedTools` (#194, `withoutCrossSessionTools`); the memory block reaches Claude Code as `## Platform memory` and the `CapabilityReport` lists `memory.runtime` (CLAUDE.md, settings — loaded from the folder with `settingSources: ['project']`, #461, but the project's, not platform memory) as unsupported (MEM-10). macOS Keychain behaviour is unverified, so v1 validates Windows only; `doctor()` implements EXE-07 with the codes in `CLAUDE_CODE_DOCTOR_CODES` (`shared-config-dir` is the error). Isolation is pinned by `packages/runtimes/__tests__/claude-code/isolation.test.ts` (three profiles: every child env equal to the others except its own `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, a session in one profile changing nothing about another, a shared config dir refused and its verdict failing exactly the two) and AC-02 by the routing test "multi-account environments on one machine" (three environments selectable through Routing, every `session.open` naming the chosen one, accounts and verdicts untouched by execution); `docs/multi-account.md` has the mechanism, the verified / unverified matrix and the manual checklist for the real CLI (#43).
- Shared harness pieces (#318, `packages/runtimes/src/harness/`): `bridgedPlatformTools`, `openDaemonConnectors` / `withConnectorPolicy`, `profileEnv` (strip the parent's account variables, set the profile's home), `withPlatformMemoryLabel` / `withUnavailableConnectors`, `harnessCapabilityReport(subject, caps)` and the session guards (`assertCwdInRoots`, `assertRuntime`, `closingWith`). Claude Code uses them; the Copilot CLI (`copilot-cli`, `COPILOT_HOME`) and Codex (`codex-cli`, `CODEX_HOME`) drivers reuse them (#317).
- Copilot CLI (#319, `@agentic/runtimes/copilot-cli`): `copilotCliDriver` runs one `@github/copilot-sdk` client per environment, with `COPILOT_HOME = profileDir` and the parent's GitHub token variables stripped. Its `copilotCli` agent maps Copilot session events onto the event wire. Copilot's permission requests go to the daemon-compiled policy. Platform tools and connectors are native client tools, ruled on in their handler. The system prompt is appended; repository instructions and config discovery are on (#461), and platform memory stays labelled as the platform's (MEM-10). Resume is `local`, by Copilot session id. `copilotCliQuota` probes `account.getQuota`, which gives monthly premium, chat and completion requests. The doctor codes are `COPILOT_CLI_DOCTOR_CODES`, and `shared-login` flags a profile running on the machine's `gh` keyring login.
- Codex (#320, `@agentic/runtimes/codex-cli`): `codexCliDriver` drives one `codex app-server` per environment over stdio JSON-RPC (`CODEX_HOME` = `profileDir`, the daemon's `OPENAI_*` removed) and one Codex thread per session. The system prompt goes in as developer instructions, with memory labelled as the platform's (MEM-10). Session tools are served on a loopback MCP server named in the thread's config as `mcp_servers.agentic`, and each call is gated by the session policy. Approval requests map to `accept` / `acceptForSession` / `decline`, and cancel is `turn/interrupt`, steer `turn/steer`. `inspect` and the quota probe (`codexCliQuota`: `account/read` plus `account/rateLimits/read`, primary → Current session, secondary → Current week) run on a short-lived app-server and never read token files. Streamed `account/rateLimits/updated` is re-emitted as `ext codex-cli/rate-limits`. Doctor codes are in `CODEX_CLI_DOCTOR_CODES` (`shared-home` is the error).
- **Harness packages** (#369; EXE-08, PLG-02, PLG-09): the runtimes' native builds are not in the daemon zip (≈ 330 MB → ≈ 8 MB). They ship as separate release assets, and the daemon installs them into its own store.
  - **Packages.** `scripts/package.mjs --harness <runtime>` builds `harness-<runtime>-<version>-<os>-<arch>.zip` (the version in the name, so a zip is uploaded once per pinned version, #441): the runtime's native npm package for that platform, at the version the lockfile pins (the SDK's own, which pins its native package), laid out as `node_modules/<package>/…`, plus `manifest.json { runtime, version, platform, binary, packages, sha256 }`. `sha256` is the tree digest (`treeHash`: over `<path>\0<file sha256>\n` in path order); `binary` is the executable. `resolveClosure` leaves every platform's native package out (`isNativeHarnessPackage`). The release workflow builds a runtime's zips on every matrix OS when its pinned version is not on `daemon-latest` yet (all three on a tag; `manifest.mjs plan` / `stale`), and `manifest.mjs` lists them as `ReleaseManifest.harnesses[runtime] = { version, assets: { '<os>-<arch>': ReleaseAsset } }`. The table of SDK, native package and executable per runtime is `scripts/lib/harness.mjs` for packaging and `BUILTIN_HARNESSES` in `apps/daemon/src/harness.ts` for the daemon; `package.test.ts` keeps the two equal.
  - **Store and locator.** The store (`harnessStore`) keeps `<install root>/harnesses/<runtime>/<version>/` and `<runtime>/current.json { version, installedAt }`. `locate(runtime) → { version, dir, binary, source, installedAt } | undefined`.
    - Without `current.json` it falls back to the native package beside the SDK in the daemon's own `node_modules` (`source: 'bundled'`): a workspace checkout, never the release zip.
    - `state()` tells ready, missing and broken apart: `current.json` naming a version that is not there, a manifest that does not match, or a missing executable.
    - `stage` downloads a `ReleaseAsset`, checks its sha256 and size, unpacks it one entry at a time (`extractZipFile`; an entry leaving the directory is refused), and checks the runtime, version, platform and tree digest. It lands in `<version>/` beside the current one; `activate` switches `current.json` and `prune` removes the rest.
    - `hello.harnesses` / `harnesses` are its `reports()`. `current` there means the version this daemon's SDK pins.
  - **Drivers** (`builtinRuntimes({ harnesses })`). Each driver runs the located executable, and a runtime without a harness is still registered, as `harnessMissingDriver`: `unknown` sign-in, no capabilities, a `harness-missing` doctor error, and `open` throws `HarnessMissingError`, which the daemon answers `session.closed { code: 'harness-missing' }`. `rebuild(runtime)` builds a driver again; the quota sources are stable wrappers over whichever build is current. `doctor` names each harness and, for a missing one, a `claude` / `copilot` / `codex` on `PATH` — a hint only: the daemon never runs it.
  - **How each SDK is pointed at its executable (the spike, #369).**
    - Claude Code: the Agent SDK's `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` → `claude(.exe)` at the root of `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk-<os>-<arch>`, also passed to the quota probe.
    - Codex: `codexPath` → `vendor/<target triple>/bin/codex(.exe)` of `@openai/codex-<os>-<arch>`, which is what `@openai/codex`'s launcher would run. `codexCommand` spawns a native path directly.
    - Copilot: `@github/copilot-sdk` 1.0.14 does have an override. `CopilotClient` takes `connection: RuntimeConnection.forStdio({ path })`, and honours `COPILOT_CLI_PATH` in its `env`, before it would resolve `@github/copilot-sdk-<os>-<arch>` beside itself (`ensureRuntimeBundle`). The spawned `prebuilds/<os>-<arch>/copilot-runtime(.exe)` loads `runtime.node` and the auth libraries next to it. So the harness carries only the platform package, the SDK's JavaScript (and `koffi`) stays in the daemon zip, and no `createRequire(<harness dir>)` is needed. The seam is `CopilotClientInit.cliPath` in `copilot-cli/sdk.ts`: `loadCopilotClient` turns it into the stdio connection, and the daemon passes it through the driver's existing `createClient`. `copilot-runtime` is not a user CLI (it refuses `login`), so `env login` for Copilot still runs `copilot` on `PATH`; Claude Code and Codex sign in with the harness executable.
  - **`harness.request`** (the `harness` feature). Requests run one at a time.
    - `install` / `update` emit `downloading`, `verifying` and `staged` from the store.
    - They then drain only that runtime. Its turn-starting prompts get `drainingReply` and a new `session.open` on it gets `code: 'draining'`, while other runtimes run on. The drain waits for the runtime's running turns, up to `HARNESS_DRAIN_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 min), or not at all with `mode: 'now'`. The `draining` phase is sent only when the runtime has live sessions.
    - `applying` closes its live sessions with `code: 'harness-update'` through the same `closeSession` as `stop`, disposes the driver, activates the version, rebuilds the driver and re-inspects: `env` goes out only when a descriptor changed. The old version is then pruned.
    - It ends with `done` and a `harnesses` frame. The platform re-opens the sessions from `spec.resume` (#420).
    - `remove` is refused `in-use` while any environment's runtime is the one named, and `not-installed` when the store has none. Failures are named: `invalid`, `download-failed`, `checksum`, `invalid-package`, `in-use`, `not-installed`, `io`, `unsupported`.
  - **`agentic-daemon harness list | install | update | rm`** (`harness-cli.ts`) uses the same store and reads the installers' manifests. By default a stable build reads its own `daemon-v<version>` release, anything else `daemon-latest`; `--version`, `--channel` / `AGENTIC_CHANNEL`, `--manifest` and `AGENTIC_RELEASES` override that. It does not drain or prune: a running daemon takes a terminal install on its next start, and prunes unused versions then (a live hand-off is #444). The installers install `AGENTIC_HARNESSES` (default all three, `none` for none) from the manifest they read, through the zip's `install.* --harness … --manifest …`.
  - **Selection and the heal on start: the migration.** A machine installed after #364 but before this change runs a daemon whose zip bundles the runtimes, and it takes updates. Updated to a build without them, it would start with no `<root>/harnesses/*`, and every runtime would be `harness-missing`; an `auto-when-idle` policy would do that unattended. So the daemon heals itself.
    - `<root>/harnesses/selection.json { runtimes }` records what the machine wants. The installers write `AGENTIC_HARNESSES` there with `harness select` (`none` → `[]`); `harness install` adds to it and `harness rm` takes out.
    - A **missing** file means every built-in runtime. That is the case for an updated machine from before the selection existed.
    - `run` under an install root passes `harnesses.heal = { manifestUrl }`: its own channel's manifest (`DAEMON_CHANNEL` `stable` → `daemon-stable`, `latest` / `dev` → `daemon-latest`; `AGENTIC_RELEASES` honoured).
    - After `start`, queued with the harness requests, the daemon installs every selected runtime it has a driver for whose harness is not ready. Each goes through the same `stage` → `activate` → driver rebuild as `harness.request`, logs, and sends a `harnesses` frame (and `env`, when the verdicts change). Until then `session.open` on that runtime is refused `harness-missing`.
    - **Stale harnesses (#600).** The same pass updates a runtime whose store harness is ready but is not the version the build pins (`store.pinned`, the SDK version it was built with; `current: false` in `harnesses`). Without this, a daemon update carried a new pin (Agent SDK 0.3.280, Opus 5.5) while the machine kept running the old harness. It goes through `applyStaged`, the drain `harness.request update` uses: that runtime's turns drain, its sessions close `harness-update` and are re-opened from `spec.resume`, and the old version is pruned. A harness outside the store (bundled) is left alone. A current one does not fetch the manifest.
    - A failure (no manifest, no build for this platform, a bad download) is logged and recorded in `<root>/harnesses/failures.json`. `doctor` shows it (`harness-install-failed`), the next start tries again, and a successful install clears it. It is never fatal.
- Platform tools reach Claude Code through the adapter's local MCP tool server; their `execute` sends `tool.call` over the same socket and awaits `tool.result`.
- **Daemon** (`apps/daemon`, #19; the lifecycle: tracking #358, decisions 2026-09-21). Operating it: `docs/runbook.md` §5.
  - **Pairing and the socket.** `agentic-daemon pair <code> --url` posts to `POST /auth/pair` and stores `{url, workspaceId, machineId, token}` in `%APPDATA%/agentic/credentials.json`, owner-only (`icacls /inheritance:r /grant:r user:F` before the token is written; `0600` elsewhere). `run` dials `/_agentic/daemon/{machineId}` with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (never in the URL), redialling with exponential backoff and jitter; every log line is redacted of the token.
  - **Session log and ref.** The session log is keyed by the **platform** session id (`ndjsonEventLog(dir).forSession(sessionId)` as the `serveSession` `eventLog`) — a runtime names its sessions its own way. `session.opened.head` is the served head, `(0, 0)` before any event — except on a re-open (below); `hello.resume` is the later of the head and the last frame sent. `session.opened.ref` is whatever the runtime calls the session before its first prompt — the Claude Code adapter's `ref` is a live getter whose id is a `cc_…` placeholder until the CLI's `system/init` event names it — so the daemon remembers the ref the platform last heard per session and, after every frame its pump sends (and at each pump start, which covers a reconnect), reads `session.ref` again and sends `session.ref {sessionId, ref}` when the identity (`id`, `data.epoch`, never the object) changed (#389). It is never tied to `turn-end`: a turn that errors before it ends still named the session. The title is (#460): after every `turn-end` seen off the served stream (`watchTurns`, so it lands while the platform is away and goes out at the next pump start), the daemon asks the driver's `title()` and sends `session.title {sessionId, title}` when it differs from the one the platform last heard; a turn that brought no new title is followed by one re-probe `TITLE_RECHECK_MS` (10 s) later, since a CLI titles from a background call that a short first turn outruns; a session re-opened from a ref is probed once at open. A probe that fails is logged, never the session's problem. Because `serveSession` replays whatever the store holds when its buffer has moved on, the daemon's pump checks each frame follows the last (next seq, or seq 1 of a later epoch) and sends a `gap {from: wanted, resumeAt}` before a hole. `session.open` is refused with a reason for an unknown environment, a runtime without a driver (or without a harness, `harness-missing`), a `cwd` outside `cwdRoots`, or a full environment (`concurrency`).
  - **Close codes and re-open** (#363; OPS-04/05/06, EXE-12). `Daemon.stop({ reason: 'stop' | 'restart' | 'update' | 'harness-update' })` closes each live session while the socket is up with `session.closed { code: reason }` (no code for a plain `stop`), then flushes the logs; `run` maps SIGINT / SIGTERM to `restart` and `until → 'update'` to `update` (the update client), and `harness-update` is the harness store's (#369). On Windows `Stop-ScheduledTask` kills without a signal, so the coded close is best-effort there — the platform treats an uncoded close the same (#420). A wanted session the daemon no longer runs (restart) is replayed from its log, then `session.closed` — with `code: 'restart'` when a log shows the machine ran it, as `unknown session` with no code otherwise. The platform owns resume: it re-opens a lost session under the same id with `spec.resume = session.ref`, and the daemon keeps **no session manifests**. The driver re-opens the runtime conversation (every driver forwards `spec.resume`; one that throws is answered `session.closed { code: 'resume-failed' }` and the platform starts fresh), the served session appends to the same NDJSON log, and `session.opened.head` is `(epoch, 0)` of the runtime's later epoch — the ref's `data.epoch`, which every driver reports, or else the log head's epoch + 1 — so it continues after the log's head. `serveSession` has no seam for an initial head, so the daemon keeps that base per live session (`LiveSession.base`) and reads the later of it and the served head (`headOf`); an upstream `serveSession({ head })` would remove it (`docs/promotion.md`). `inMemoryHarness()` does the same (`restart()`, the `resume` conformance feature) and `session-reopen` passes for both.
  - **Background service and the supervisor** (#362, fixes #353). Background running on Windows is a per-user Scheduled Task (`apps/daemon/scripts/install-service.ps1`), not a LocalSystem service, because the token and every `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` belong to the user. The task (and the launchd agent / systemd unit, `install-service.sh`) runs the **supervisor** (`scripts/supervise.mjs`, dependency-free, copied to `<install root>/supervisor/` so a swap never replaces it), not the daemon: it relaunches `run` after any exit with a backoff (1 s → 60 s), stops on exit `0`, and on exit `75` (`EXIT_UPDATE`) swaps `daemon.staged` in, keeping `daemon.prev`; a swapped-in version that does not write `state/ready` within 90 s or exits twice within 2 min is rolled back with `state/update-failed.json` (`not-ready` / `crashed` / `swap-failed: …`). `state/supervisor.json` carries `restarts` and `lastExit`; `state/supervisor.log` every exit, swap and rollback. The daemon side: `DaemonOptions.onWelcome` → `run` writes `state/ready` on the first `welcome`; every `run` exit logs `daemon: exiting { reason, code }` (`ExitReason`; `uncaughtException` / `unhandledRejection` handlers exit 1); `readSupervisorState` reads the supervisor's files at start and logs them. The install root is `installPaths()` in `src/paths.ts` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\agentic` / `~/.agentic`, `AGENTIC_INSTALL_DIR`).
  - **Update client** (#364; EXE-08/09, OPS-04/05/06, `apps/daemon/src/update.ts`). `hello` carries `build` (`version`, `commit`, `protocol`, `channel`, `platform: platformKey(process.platform, process.arch)`), `features` (`update` when the daemon runs under the supervisor — `state/supervisor.json` and the `AGENTIC_INSTALL_DIR` it sets; otherwise an `update.request` is answered `failed { code: 'unsupported' }` — composed with `harness`), and `restarts` / `lastExit` / `lastUpdate` mapped from the supervisor's files (`helloLifecycle` in `cli.ts`; `update-failed.json` is deleted after the first `welcome`, so a rollback is reported by one process). An `update.request` runs one at a time (another, or a staged folder already there, is `failed { code: 'busy' }`): `downloading` (`https:` only — a test injects `allowLoopbackHttp`, never production — to `<root>/downloads/<version>.zip` via `.part`, hashed as it arrives, `progress` at most every 2 s, 10-minute timeout) → `verifying` (sha256 and bytes; a mismatch deletes the file, `checksum`) → `staged` (unpacked through `daemon.staged.part` into `daemon.staged`, which must hold `install.*` and `bin/agentic-daemon.mjs`, then `node daemon.staged/bin/agentic-daemon.mjs --version` once, `bad-package` / `unrunnable`) → `draining` → `restarting`. `target: 'previous'` renames `daemon.prev` to `daemon.staged` (`no-previous` when absent; a `daemon.staged.previous` marker lets a cancel or a crash put it back). Drain is no new **turns**: while draining, `Daemon` answers a turn-starting `prompt` with `drainingReply` and still accepts `session.open`; it ends when no turn runs on any environment (the `running` flags `runningOn` counts), when `drainTimeoutMs` passes, or at once for `mode: 'now'`. `restarting` calls `UpdateClientOptions.restart`, which `run` wires to its `until` (`'update'`): `Daemon.stop({ reason: 'update' })` closes what is live with `session.closed { code: 'update' }`, `state/ready` is deleted and `run` returns 75, so the supervisor swaps. `update.cancel` before `restarting` aborts the download or the drain, unstages and answers `failed { code: 'cancelled' }`; after it, it is ignored. On start the client removes a leftover `daemon.staged`, `daemon.staged.part` and `downloads/*.part`. `agentic-daemon update [--channel stable|latest] [--version daemon-v…] [--check] [--now]` (`update-cli.ts`) reads the manifest the installers read (`AGENTIC_RELEASES`, `daemon-<channel>` or `daemon-v<semver>`), prints installed and available (`compareVersions`: newer, the installed version, or older), and — needing `state/supervisor.json` — downloads, verifies and stages with the same functions, then writes `state/update-request.json` `{ mode, drainTimeoutMs: 10 min, version, at }`; the running daemon's client polls for it and runs `draining` → `restarting` without telling the platform, and the CLI waits for `state/ready` to name the new version (or reports a rollback, or a daemon that never took the request, which it unstages).
  - **Packaging, version and releases** (#52, #343, #361, #437). The daemon ships as a self-contained zip (`pnpm --filter @agentic/daemon package` → `scripts/package.mjs`): built `bin/` + `dist/`, the production dependency closure laid out as a plain `node_modules/` (workspace packages as `dist/`, the runtime SDKs' JavaScript without any native package — ≈ 8 MB; the native builds are the harness packages below), `install.ps1` / `install.sh` (Node ≥ 22.12 check → `pair` → `harness install` → `doctor` → the launcher → the service) / `uninstall.*` / README at the root; the machine needs only Node, which the one-line bootstraps download when missing. `agentic-daemon --version` prints `agentic-daemon <version> (<commit>, protocol <n>, <channel>)` — `DAEMON_VERSION` / `DAEMON_COMMIT` / `DAEMON_CHANNEL`, stamped at build time by `scripts/lib/stamp.mjs`: a `daemon-v<semver>` tag → `<semver>`, `stable`; otherwise `<package version>-main.<commit unix seconds>.<sha7>` (a digits-only sha7 with a leading zero written `g<sha7>`), `latest`, so main builds order by commit time and below their release. Each release carries a `manifest.json` (`scripts/lib/manifest.mjs`, `ReleaseManifest`) at a fixed URL — the versioned `daemon-v<semver>` and the rolling `daemon-latest` / `daemon-stable` — which the installers, `update`, `harness` and the platform's `ReleaseDirectory` read; never GitHub's "latest release" (runbook §5.1). `__tests__/package.test.ts` unpacks the zip, keeps it under 50 MB and runs it on plain Node.
  - **Quota** (#271). `QuotaMonitor` (`apps/daemon/src/quota.ts`) runs the build's `QuotaSource`s (`builtinQuotaSources()`, one per runtime). It taps each live session event once per cursor (a reconnect replay is not news): `ext` → `fromSignal`, and `error` as `{ns: 'error', name: code}`. Unless `--quota-probe off`, it also probes after `welcome`, polls idle environments every `quota.pollMs` (5 min), probes 30 s after a `turn-end`, and probes at once after a rejection (`error rate_limited` with an exhausted window, #452: the stream names one window, the probe the per-model ones), one probe at a time. It merges with `mergeQuota` and sends `quota {environmentId, snapshot}`. An unchanged snapshot is dropped unless the last send is older than 15 min, since the platform judges staleness from `observedAt`.
  - **Telemetry** (#400). `createTelemetrySampler` (`apps/daemon/src/telemetry.ts`) runs off the heartbeat's tick (never two samples at once): one process-table read per tick (`ps -axo pid=,ppid=,rss=,time=` on macOS and Linux, `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` through PowerShell on Windows; `vm_stat` on macOS for the memory in use — `os.freemem()` there is free pages only), then each root's subtree by parent links is charged to it: a session's root is the pid its runtime reports (`OpenedRuntimeSession.pid` — the Claude Code driver runs each session's `prompt` in an `AsyncLocalStorage` context and reads it in the agent-level `spawn`, so the CLI process the adapter starts lazily in the first prompt is that session's; `pids.ts`), an environment's roots are `RuntimeDriver.pids(envId)` (Codex's app-server, `CodexConnection.pid`), the daemon is its own row only. CPU is the tree's cumulative CPU time between two samples over the wall time and the cores (`null` on the first; a pid seen with less time than before is a reused number and measures nothing); RSS is the tree's sum. A session without a root reads `null`; an environment is `'session'` (its sessions' sum, or `null` when none has a process yet), `'environment'` or `'none'` (Copilot: the SDK spawns its own runtime); `availability` is `partial` with any `null`, `not-reported` with the reason when the table could not be read or `--telemetry off`. The frame is `telemetry {snapshot}`, a full replace.
- **Machine actor** (`packages/platform/src/machine`, #36): `defineMachineActor({ socket, sessions, tools?, heartbeatWindowMs?, commandTimeoutMs? })`, keyed `{ws}:machine:{id}`, save persistence. `pair(code, info)` redeems the code through `Workspace.claimPairing` over a hop and stores only the token hash (`tokenRecord()` is what `authenticate`'s `machines` lookup and the socket handshake verify against; `revoke()` refuses the token from then on and closes the socket). The host owns the socket: on Cloudflare (`apps/web/src/daemon`) the Worker forwards `/_agentic/daemon/{machineId}` to the Machine's own Durable Object, which verifies `Authorization: Bearer amt.…` against `tokenRecord`, accepts the server end with the hibernation API under the `agentic:daemon` tag, and hands every text message to `socketMessage(raw)` under the machine principal; a close or error calls `socketClosed()` (offline at once). Outbound frames go through the `MachineSocketPort` (actor key → `state.getWebSockets(tag)`). `hello` records os / version / environments / capabilities, answers `welcome {wanted}` from the cursor the machine last forwarded per hosted session (`{0,0}` when none), re-sends `session.open` for sessions the daemon never acknowledged and every pending command (replies are idempotent by `commandId`); `env` replaces the environments; `heartbeat` / `pong` refresh `lastSeen`; `telemetry` (#400) replaces `telemetry` whole (`pruneTelemetry` keeps only hosted sessions and reported environments — also on `hello`, `env` and every session closure), then `telemetryWarnings` (core: a session over 2 GiB resident, the machine over 90 % in use; CPU is never warned) tells the Inbox `resource-pressure` once per key (`telemetryWarned`), and again only after `telemetryWarningCleared` (under 80 % of the limit, or the session gone) — never an automatic close (#387). `get()` carries `telemetry`. The stored descriptors carry the daemon's per-environment `doctor` verdict, and `doctor(environmentId?)` → `MachineDoctorView { ok, unverified, environments: [{ environmentId, name, runtime, account, isolation, verdict? }] }` returns them as stored (never recomputed — the platform cannot see a config dir): `ok` only when every environment has a verdict and none is an error; this is the `environments.doctor` shape for the MCP surface (#50) and the Machines page (#90). `openSession(sessionId, environmentId, spec, {taskId?})` → `opened` when the environment has a free slot (`concurrency.max` minus the sessions running a turn, #394 — see "Capacity counts running turns" below) or `queued` until a turn ends there — idempotent by id: a session the machine already hosts answers `opened` with no new `session.open` and no `session.opened` to follow, so the router prompts a reused one itself (#393); `closeSession` sends `session.close` (or drops a queued one); `session.opened` binds the session and forwards a synthesized wire `hello` (capabilities, since the daemon's pump skips it; the ref it carries is the open's placeholder and the Session ignores it) to the Session; `session.ref` updates the hosted session's `ref` and forwards it as `Session.noteRef` (#389); `session.frame` → `Session.forwardFrames`, `session.reply` → `Session.commandReplied`, `session.closed` frees the slot, answers that session's pending commands `closed` and dequeues the next; `tool.call` runs on the `ToolCallPort` under `mintAgentPrincipal({ws, agentId, sessionId, taskId?})`, detached from the socket turn, and answers `tool.result`. `sendCommand(sessionId, command)` (the app binds the Session's `CommandSink` to it) records `pending {sessionId, command, sentAt, deadline}` and sends while online; the `liveness` reminder (60 s floor) marks a machine offline after `heartbeatWindowMs` (90 s) of silence and answers commands past their deadline (120 s) with `error internal`. Session hops are fresh `actor()` calls under the machine principal (never `ctx.actor`): the daemon-path entry points admit only the hosting machine, and `sendCommand` may run inside the Session's own turn — the not-hosted `closed` reply is `oneWay` so it never waits on the turn waiting on it. With #37 the machine also tells the Routing actor — one-way, as itself, when `MachinePorts.routing` is set — `machineOnline` on every `hello`, `sessionOpened` when the daemon acknowledges a hosted session (a dequeued one included), `sessionClosed` when one is gone, so a task parked on the machine resumes or fails with the daemon's reason (a session refused before it opened fails its task `session-refused`) — the router applies either to every route still waiting on that session, since a chat's session serves many tasks and the task the machine names is only the one it was opened for (#393) — and, with #394, `slotFreed(machineId, environmentId, why)` when a turn ends in an environment (or a session running one closes) and `promptRefused(sessionId, turnId, code, message)` when the daemon answers a prompt with an error; the anonymous `POST /auth/pair` resolves the code through the global `PairingDirectory` (filed by `Workspace.registerMachinePending`, single use) and then calls `Machine.pair(code, info)` as that machine. A `session.closed` for a session the daemon had opened (#420) goes to its record first — `Session.hostEnded({ reason, code? })`, as the machine and before `sessionClosed` reaches the router; a refusal or a failure there is logged and never takes the socket down — so a turn still running there is interrupted instead of left `running` for a session nobody hosts (§7 "A daemon restart mid-turn"). With #366 the frame's `code` (`SessionClosedCode`) travels with it, and a close while the entry is still `opening` — with `resume-failed`, or for a re-open from a ref (`spec.resume`) — is a refused re-open: `hostEnded({ code: 'resume-failed' })` closes the record, ref and all, so the chat's next message binds a fresh session. `socketClosed` and the liveness reminder's heartbeat check tell the router `machineOffline(machineId)` when the machine goes from online to offline, and it hears `machineOnline` on the way back — on `hello`, and on a heartbeat that ends a silent spell on the same socket (#524: without it, a machine the heartbeat window marked offline read online again while its tasks waited `machine-offline` until they failed `machine-lost`); `closeSession` on a machine that is offline lets the session go at once (`sessionGone`: the record is told its host ended) — the router's `machine-lost` uses it.
- **Daemon updates** (#365, `machine/update.ts`, `releases/`): with `MachinePorts.releases` (the global `ReleaseDirectory`) and `inbox` wired, `hello` stores the daemon's `build` / `features` / `restarts` / `lastExit` / `harnesses`, reads the Workspace's `settings.updates` and the directory (a hop each, before `welcome`, which now carries `platform {version, minDaemonVersion, latest {stable, latest}}`), and compares: `available` is the channel's manifest when `compareVersions(manifest.version, build.version) > 0` (with the asset for `build.platform`), and a version seen for the first time is one `update-available` Inbox row (ref `{kind: 'machine'}`). A daemon with no `build` is compared with nothing and cannot be updated: `requestUpdate` answers 409 "reinstall once" (so does one without `features: ['update']`). `requestUpdate({target?, mode, drainTimeoutMs?})` (owner only) sends `update.request` with the asset from the directory, holds `update.pending` and sets `draining`; `update.status` frames move `pending.phase / progress`. The next `hello` judges it: the version asked for → `applied`, `machine.updated`, Inbox `update-applied`; another version, or a `lastUpdate` `rolled-back` since the request → `failed` / `rolled-back`, `machine.update-failed`, Inbox `update-failed`. A `failed` phase, `cancelUpdate` (sends `update.cancel`) and the liveness tick past `deadline` (`drainTimeoutMs` + 10 min, `timeout`, by `system:updates`) close it too. **Drain:** idle means no running turns (`isIdle`, a live session with no turn does not count); while `draining` covers an environment `freeSlots` is 0 there, so a prompt parks `waiting-capacity` on the router's unchanged path, while `openSession` / `dequeue` still open sessions (`openRoom` — `freeSlots` without the drain; an open costs no slot since #394). The drain ends on every `hello`, on a `failed` phase, on `cancelUpdate` and on the timeout; then `dequeue()` runs and the router hears `slotFreed` for every environment. **Policy** (`nextAutoUpdate`): `auto-when-idle` asks as soon as the machine is idle, `window` only inside `[occurrence, occurrence + durationMs)` of its cron (`recur.ts`'s `nextOccurrence`) and idle; never while pending, never again for a version whose last attempt failed; evaluated on `hello`, when a turn ends (`slotFreed`) and on the liveness tick (which re-reads the directory and the workspace settings at most every 15 min) — never on `session.closed`; the request is `system:updates`. `hello.restarts` deltas feed a 10-minute log: 3 restarts in it is one `daemon-crash-loop` row per window. `updateState()` also reports `impact {runningTurns, liveSessions}` for the web confirm (#367). None of the five methods, nor `update.request` / `harness.request`, is on a tool surface (`tool-boundary.test.ts`). **Restart from the web (#355, #481):** `requestRestart({mode, drainTimeoutMs?})` (owner only, not elevated — a restart narrows nothing) rides the same machinery with `update.request { target: 'restart' }` under `requestUpdate`'s guards (409 while an update or a harness change is pending, or without `features: ['update']`): the daemon downloads and stages nothing, drains (or not, `now`), closes its sessions `session.closed { code: 'restart' }` and exits 75 with no `daemon.staged` — the supervisor's plain relaunch; the next `hello`, whatever build it names, closes the pending record `restarted` and records `machine.restarted` (no Inbox row: the owner asked a moment ago; `machine.restart-requested` at the ask). A restart counts toward the supervisor's `restarts` and so toward `daemon-crash-loop`, like an update. **The daemon's log (#481):** `logTail(lines = 200)` (owner only; 409 without `features: ['log']`) sends `log.request` and keeps a `logRequests` record (≤ 16, 60 s TTL, `timeout` at `envTimeoutMs`, on disconnect and revoke); `log.response` turns it `done` with the lines held by the activation — never on the record, like history — and `logResult(requestId)` reads them (an answer that landed on another activation reads `error io`; the page asks again). The daemon tails `<stateDir>/logs/daemon.log` (`DaemonPaths.logFile`, what the service definitions write) through the `logTail` port `cli.ts` injects: the last 512 KiB at most, the first cut line dropped, every line redacted again; a foreground `run` has no file and answers `no-log`.
- **Harnesses** (#370, `machine/actor.ts`; EXE-08, AGT-09, OPS-03, PLG-02, PLG-09): `hello.harnesses` and every `harnesses` frame replace `harnesses`. The compare that sets `update.available` (on `hello`, `setChannel` and the liveness tick) also fills `harnessesAvailable` from the channel manifest's `harnesses[runtime]`, with the asset for `build.platform`; an installed runtime older than the one shipped is one `harness-update-available` Inbox row per runtime and version (`harnessesNotified`). `requestHarness({op, runtime, version?, mode = 'drain'})` (owner only) takes the asset from the directory (the channel's version, or `version` from either channel), sends `harness.request` and keeps a request record (the `envRequest` pattern: ≤ 16, pruned 2 min after it ends, read live with `harnessResult`). Refusals: 400 a malformed input, an unknown version, no build for the platform, the version already installed; 403 revoked; 503 offline or no directory; 409 a daemon without `features: ['harness']` ("reinstall once"), another harness request or a daemon update pending (one at a time — the daemon runs them in order and the machine holds one drain; `requestUpdate` is 409 while a harness request runs), `update` of a runtime not installed and `remove` `not-installed`, and `remove` `in-use` while an environment of the machine runs on the runtime (the daemon checks too). **Drain per runtime:** while the request is pending `draining` is `{requestId, since, runtime}`, so `freeSlots` is 0 in that runtime's environments only — a prompt there parks `waiting-capacity`, every other runtime keeps running — and opens still go out. `harness.status` moves `phase`; `done` / `failed` end the record, record `harness.changed` (`from` the version installed when asked, `to` the one asked for) and end the drain (`slotFreed` everywhere); so does the deadline (`HARNESS_DEADLINE_MS`, 30 min: `timeout`). A `hello` fails a pending request `interrupted` (and, like every `hello`, ends the drain); a late `done` / `failed` over `timeout` / `interrupted` still lands. The daemon closes the runtime's sessions with `harness-update` and the router re-opens them (#420).
- **Capacity counts running turns, not open sessions** (#394; EXE-03, EXE-09, EXE-11). Once a chat member's session lives for the chat's life (#393), counting open sessions would let the first member own its environment and refuse every other chat, delegated child and scheduled run, and would make the environment impossible to remove. So `concurrency` bounds the turns in flight. It is optional and absent by default (#694): an environment without it has no limit — the descriptor carries `concurrency: { active }` with no `max`, `freeSlots` is unbounded, and the daemon never answers `busy` for it. The web sets a limit, or clears it (`EnvironmentInput.concurrency: null`), from the environment's Edit dialog. On the daemon (`apps/daemon/src/daemon.ts`) each `LiveSession` carries `running`: set when a `prompt` command is taken (tentatively while its reply is out, kept on the ack), cleared when the turn's `turn-end` passes a per-session watcher on the served stream (`watchTurns` — off the socket pump, so a turn that ends while the platform is away still frees its slot). `session.open` is refused `at capacity` only when `env.concurrency` turns are running; a `prompt` that would start one beyond that is answered with the wire `busy` error before the runtime sees it (a session already running a turn is left to the runtime — a steer, or its own `busy`); the descriptor's `concurrency.active` reports running turns; the quota probe's "idle" is "no turn running". The Machine mirrors it: `HostedSession.running { turnId, since }` is set by a prompt's ack in `onSessionReply` — or by a `turn-start` the runtime began on its own (#510, below) in `onSessionFrame` — and cleared by the turn's `turn-end` in `onSessionFrame`, and `activeIn` / `freeSlots` (`machine/state.ts`) count the hosted sessions that are running a turn or have a prompt pending — a slot is taken when the prompt leaves, not when its ack lands, so two prompts sent back to back cannot share one. A turn ending dequeues the next queued `session.open` and tells the router `slotFreed`. `removeEnvironment` refuses `409 in-use` only while a turn runs there or a session is queued for it, naming them (session, agent, turn); the idle hosted sessions are sent `session.close` first and the `env.request` follows on the same socket, so the daemon (which still refuses removal while it hosts any session) sees them gone — their chats re-open a fresh session wherever their member runs next (EXE-12). Per-session CPU and memory ride the `telemetry` frame (#400, above); capacity is admission only.
- **A turn the runtime starts itself** (#510; EXE-03, EXE-09, COL-05, OPS-05). Claude Code starts a model turn nobody prompted when background work it waited on finishes (a Plan or review subagent, a backgrounded command); `@sigx/ai-agent-claude-code` 0.2 reports it as an *implicit turn*: a `turn-start` with a turn id the adapter minted, `input: []` and no `user-message`, then a `turn-end` at the CLI's `result`. The daemon already counts it (`watchTurns` sets `running` on any `turn-start`), so the platform does too. The Session (`forwardFrames`) records a new `turn-start` that finds nothing running and no prompt of ours out for its turn id as `running { turnId, commandId: turnId, input, implicit: true }` with no `taskId` and tells the chat `typing`; a `turn-start` whose prompt ack is still out is that prompt's turn, which the ack records under its task. From there it is an ordinary turn: its `turn-end` runs `finishTurn` (the reply is a chat message with no task, the transcript and ref refresh, learning is skipped for want of a task), `hostEnded` / an eviction cut it through `finishInterrupted`, and a budget verdict can cancel it. It works no task: its usage rows go to the Ledger with no `taskId` and charge no Task (`recordUsage` takes the turn's task explicitly, never a default), while tool calls in it keep the session's principal (`currentTaskId` falls back to the opening task as before). The Machine counts it (`runningIn`, `freeSlots`, `isIdle`), so the machine is not idle and an `auto-when-idle` update waits, and its `turn-end` dequeues and tells the router `slotFreed`. A message to the same member meanwhile finds the record running: the runtime takes it as a steer when it can; otherwise the route parks `waiting-turn`, and since no route follows an implicit turn, the router's `follow` supervisor watches it (`watchTurn`: tail the session to that turn's `turn-end`, then `turnEnded`) so the message goes out when it ends. A daemon `busy` for another member's prompt parks it `waiting-capacity` until that `slotFreed`.
- **A turn nobody asked for can't wedge a session** (#604; EXE-09, OPS-05). `@sigx/ai-agent-claude-code` 0.2.1 also opens an implicit turn when the CLI answers a control request: after `configure({ model })` → `setModel` it emits a message but never a `result`, so that turn never ends, holds the session and its environment's slot, and every later prompt parks behind it (signalxjs/ai#193).
