The `repeatThreshold`-th repetition (default 3, and each multiple) adds an instruction proposal with `requiresReview: true`; nothing applies it here. `onTaskEnd` writes a `record` per task — `verified`/`refuted` are `confidence: 'verified'` + `source: 'verification'`, `claimed`/`none` are `assumed` + `agent`, the tag says which (LRN-03) — plus a lesson when a claim was refuted. Memory proposals are applied by the plugin (`apply: false` returns them instead); `applyProposals` is the one writer and runs `assertPermissionFree` over the batch first: no permission key (`permissions`, `grants`, `allowedTools`, `policy`, …) at any depth and no field outside `NewMemoryEntry` / the instruction shape, mirrored at the type level by `PermissionFree<T>` (LRN-08). `relevantLessons(memory, { objective, tags })` is the pre-task read: lessons only, no retired entry, no lesson sharing no word with the context. Counters go through the `CorrectionLedger` port (`recordCorrection({ agentId, week, what, at })`, ISO week in UTC); the Ledger actor (#45) implements it.

## 9. Plugins, MCP, A2A, auth

- `PluginManifest {id, version, kind: runtime | connector | memory | learning | notification | trigger | a2a | project-feature, name, description, capabilities[], config: ConfigSchema, secrets?: [{name, title, description, required}], permissions: [{scope, reason}], compat}`. `ConfigSchema` is a typed JSON-Schema subset core can validate (`validateConfig`) and the UI can render; secrets are declared beside it, never inside it; `pluginReadiness(state, facts)` is the one readiness verdict the pages and the platform share (#226). v1 plugins are in-repo modules registered at build time. Registry holds enabled/config/grantedPermissions; `dependents()` lists agents/schedules referencing a plugin (AC-13). Nothing receives a secret unless `grantedPermissions` includes it (PLG-04). Notifications v1 (`packages/platform/src/notify`): the seam is `NotificationChannel {id, deliver(notification, {workspaceId, subscriptions}) → {ok, error?, expired?}}`; a `notification` plugin implements it as a `ChannelPlugin {open({config, secret}) → NotificationChannel}`, and the app passes `defineInbox({channelPlugins, registry})` (#244): per notification `Inbox.push` asks `Registry.gate()` once — however many subscribers — which notification plugins are on, and opens each this build implements with its merged config and a `secret(name)` that goes through `openSecret` as the workspace owner (enabled + granted, audited). A Registry that cannot be asked is a failed attempt on channel `plugins`; static `channels` still exist for tests. `Inbox.push` records first, then fans out through `deliverAll` — a channel that rejects or answers `ok: false` becomes a `DeliveryAttempt` on the record, never a throw (OPS-04). `webPushChannel({fetch, vapid, ttlSeconds})` POSTs one push per subscription with VAPID (RFC 8292) signed as ES256 over WebCrypto, so it is edge-safe; 404/410 endpoints are reported `expired` and the inbox drops them. **Gap:** v1 pushes are *contentless* — no RFC 8291 `aes128gcm` payload encryption — so the service worker treats a push event as "inbox changed" and re-reads `Inbox.list`; encrypted payloads (title/body in the push itself) are a follow-up. Web Push is the plugin `agentic.notify.web-push` (`webPushPlugin` + `webPushChannelPlugin`, off by default): config `{subject (required), publicKey}`, secret `vapid-private-key`. Its page's "Generate keys" makes the P-256 pair in the browser (WebCrypto), saves the public half with `configure` and seals the private half with `setSecret`; a new pair drops the Inbox's subscriptions, since browsers subscribed with the old key. A missing contact becomes `defaultContact(origin)` (the https origin, else `mailto:push@<host>`). Settings → Notifications' "Turn on push notifications" (#543) does the whole path in one click — `ensurePushKeys` (makes a pair only when there is none), `enable`, then subscribes this browser — so every further browser reuses the pair and those already subscribed keep their pushes. The channel opens the private key only when a notification has a subscription to go to, and without a contact, public key or private key it records a failed attempt naming what is missing, never an unsigned push. Browsers subscribe in Settings → Notifications (`apps/web/src/push`): `/sw.js` (`apps/web/public`) shows a generic "something needs you" notification per push — it cannot read the inbox itself — and focuses or opens the app on click. Triggers v1: HMAC-validated webhook → Task. **Runtime kinds (#313):** a `runtime` manifest declares what it is with a capability: `harness` (a product with its own loop and sign-in, driven through its CLI or SDK, e.g. Claude Code), `model` (the platform runs the loop over a model API, e.g. `anthropic-api`) or `remote` (an A2A peer). Core's `runtimeKindOf(manifest)` reads it. This is independent of `daemon-hosted` / `platform-hosted`, and `/plugins` groups runtimes by it. **Usage limits (#261)** are a runtime capability, `usage-limits`: the runtime supplies a `QuotaSource {id, version, runtime, probe?(env, ctx), fromSignal?(signal, env)}`, which is not a plugin kind. The daemon runs the build's sources (`builtinQuotaSources()`). `claudeCodeQuota()` maps a session's `rate_limit_event` to one window and probes an idle account's `/usage`; `anthropicApiQuota()` answers `not-reported` with its reason. Snapshots are normalized (`QuotaWindow`: utilization 0..1, `resetsAt`, `status`), merged with `mergeQuota`, and stored on the Machine.
- **Project feature plugins (#330 contract, #332 wiring, #335 git).** `ProjectFeatureManifest` is a manifest of kind `project-feature` with a second schema, `projectSettings`: what a project stores under `features[id]` (its presence enables the plugin for that project); `config` stays the plugin's workspace-wide settings. The code half, `ProjectFeaturePlugin`, sits beside the manifest in the catalogue: `detect(folder)` over the daemon's git badge (the project form suggests the feature when a folder is added), `beforeSession({ project, settings, taskId, chatId?, environmentId, cwd, fs })` run by the router once per task after the folder is resolved and before the session opens — may return a new `cwd` and `instructions`; a throw parks the task with the message, never a silent fallback (EXE-12) — and `instructions(ctx)` merged into the system prompt. Plugins run on the platform only and compose the daemon's generic folder operations (`list`, `worktree`, `locate`); no plugin ships daemon code or UI. The repo's identity across machines is `FsGitInfo.origin`, compared with `sameOrigin`. As wired (#332): the Registry lists a `project-feature` manifest like every other kind (multi-enable, never a slot; `assertPluginManifest` requires its `projectSettings`), `Registry.checkProjectSettings(pluginId, settings)` is what `Workspace.upsertProject` holds each `features[id]` to (400: unknown, disabled, another kind, or settings that fail the schema), and the router runs the hooks as §7 says (`RoutingPorts.projectFeatures`). `dependents()` does not yet count projects that enable a feature — deferred; disabling a feature plugin skips it at the next placement, and `upsertProject` refuses to store settings for a disabled one. The settings form may also use three optional, pure members (#621, tracking #616): `presets` (`ProjectFeaturePreset { id, label, description?, settings }` — named starting points; `applyProjectFeaturePreset` lays one over the draft, a `null` field cleared, and every field stays editable, so the platform special-cases no convention), `settingsErrors(settings)` (the problems a schema cannot express, by settings key) and `previewSettings({ project, settings, folder? })` (labelled lines saying what the settings would do for a folder of the project). A plugin may also hear that a chat left the project (#623): `onChatReleased({ project, settings, chatId, reason, environmentId, cwd, fs })`, `reason` `project-changed` (the chat was moved to another project or out of any) or `deleted` (reserved until chats can be deleted), once per folder the project has whose machine is online — an override or a pre-#702 folder on its environment, a machine's folder (#702) once, on the first environment of that machine whose roots hold it and that has no override — `cwd` that folder and `fs` its daemon. The router runs it best effort after `Chat.setProject` has moved the chat, one-way, and only for a chat no longer in that project; a throw never undoes the move, and every call is audited (`project.chat-released`) with what the plugin returned or its error.
- **Git project feature (#335, `@agentic/plugins-git`, `packages/plugins-git`).** The first project feature plugin, edge-safe over `@agentic/core` only; the web's `projectFeatureCatalogue` maps its id to `gitFeaturePlugin` and lists `gitFeatureManifest` in the Registry's catalogue (enabled by default: it acts only on a project that switches it on). Id `agentic.feature.git`; `projectSettings`: `origin` (string — the origin remote URL as git writes it, https or `git@host:path`, so no `format: 'uri'`; the project form fills it from the first folder's badge, `identityOf(folder)` reads it, `sameOrigin` compares it), `worktreePerChat` (boolean, default false), `branchPrefix` (default `chat/`), `branchTemplate`, `worktreePath`, `reuseExisting` (default true), `worktreeNotice` (all four #619, below), `worktreeStrategy` (default `builtin`), `worktreeCreate`, `worktreeSetup` (all three #620, below), `base` (optional start point), `instructions` (default `''`). `detect(folder)` is the git badge's presence (repo or worktree). `instructions()` is the trimmed settings text. `beforeSession` acts only with `worktreePerChat` on and a `chatId`. Configurable per project (#619, tracking #616), so agentic imposes no one repo convention: `branchTemplate` (tokens `{branchPrefix}`, `{chatId8}`, `{chatId}`, `{project}`; unset = `branchPrefix + short chat id`, `gitBranchFor`), `worktreePath` (tokens those plus `{repo}`, `{repoName}`, `{repoParent}`, `{branch}`, `{branchSlug}`; unset or `auto` = `suggestWorktreePath(cwd, branch, hostOsOfPath(cwd))`), `reuseExisting` (default true) and `worktreeNotice` (tokens `{path}`, `{branch}`; unset = `DEFAULT_WORKTREE_NOTICE`, blank = none). Every token is deterministic for a chat, so every task and environment of it lands in the same folder; an unknown token, an invalid expanded branch or a relative folder throws before any daemon round trip (`gitSettingsErrors` names the same problems for the settings form). With `reuseExisting`, a session folder other than the project's own on that environment whose listing badge is a linked worktree (one the user chose for the chat or task, made anywhere) is kept as it is, with the notice. Otherwise one `{ kind: 'worktree', repo: cwd, branch, path, base? }` op, which the daemon makes idempotent (#618: `reused`, `recreated`), and `{ cwd: <the daemon's path>, instructions: <the notice> }`. With `worktreeStrategy: 'command'` (#620) the project's own `worktreeCreate` command (a repo script, a make target) makes it instead: a `list` of the folder first (a worktree of the branch there is kept, anything else is `worktree-mismatch`), then a `run` in the project folder, then a `list` that must show a worktree of the branch. `worktreeSetup` commands `run` in order in a worktree just made or re-created, never in a reused one. Command lines are split into argv (quotes only, no shell syntax) and expanded per argument; a failing one parks the task with its output's tail. Cleanup (#623): with `worktreeCleanup: 'on-chat-leave'`, `onChatReleased` removes the chat's own worktree (the path `chatWorktreeFor` names, never a chosen one) through the daemon's `worktree-remove { repo, path, branch?, deleteBranch? }` (owner-only on the Machine; `git status` first and `dirty` for any change, then `git worktree remove` without `--force`, the main checkout never; with `deleteBranch`, `git branch -d`, so an unmerged branch stays), or with the project's `worktreeRemove` command after a clean `git status` `run`. A `run` gets its own time on top of the answer deadline, on the Machine record and in the router's `machineFs` (polled at most every 2 s once slow). The notice tells the agent it is already isolated, so a repo guide that says "create a worktree first" does not move it out of the folder Files / Changes watch. Nothing is remembered per `{chatId, environmentId}`; every daemon error — `worktree-mismatch`, `branch-exists` (the branch checked out elsewhere), `not-a-repo`, `unsupported` (a daemon older than #618 answers a second task with `branch-exists` and is parked until updated) — throws `git worktree <code>: <message>` and the router parks the task `waiting { project-feature }` (EXE-12). Push/pull, PR creation and clone are out of scope (follow-up).
- MCP client (`@agentic/mcp`): `createMcpClient({url, auth}).tools() → AnyTool[]` — Streamable HTTP over `fetch` (edge-safe, no SDK; JSON and SSE responses, `Mcp-Session-Id`, `notifications/cancelled` on abort); `@agentic/mcp/node` adds `createStdioMcpClient({command, args})` for servers the daemon spawns (via `@sigx/ai-agent-node` + `@sigx/ai-agent/harness` JSON-RPC). `tools/list` entries become `defineTool({ jsonSchema: inputSchema, annotations })` with the wire schema verbatim and hint-for-hint annotations; `isError` results throw `McpToolError` so the engine shows the model a tool error. `mcpConnector({id, transport, url | command, secret?, headerSecrets? | secrets?, envSecrets?})` is the `PluginManifest` (kind connector) with `network:<host>` / `machine:<id>`, `secret:<name>` and `tools:<namespace>` permissions declared up front; every credential is a declared `manifest.secrets` entry and never config (the config holds only `url`, or `command` / `args` / `cwd`). `mcpConnectorSetup(options)` → `{ manifest, connector }` is what the app stores to add a server: `Registry.register(manifest, { enabled, grant: 'declared' })`, `Registry.putConnector(connector)` (same id — one server is one plugin; `auth` binds each secret name to the bearer, a header or an environment variable), then `Registry.setSecret(name, value)` per secret. **Adding one from the UI (#241):** `/plugins` → "Add MCP server" (`apps/web/src/pages/plugins/{connector.ts,AddConnectorDialog.tsx,LiveConnectorsView.tsx}`) takes a name (→ the id), a Streamable HTTP URL and a bearer token or an API-key header; the credential is sealed as `<id>.token` and bound by name. "Test connection" runs `openMcpConnector` from the page with the values still in the form (so a server that refuses browser requests fails the test and may still work for agents), and adding records what it found with `setConnectorStatus`. Remove is `Registry.remove`: `plugin-in-use` names the agents and schedules that pick it and removes only on confirm (`force`), deleting the connector's own secrets with it. stdio servers are not offered yet (#280). **Connectors on a session (#240, as built):** `Routing.run` passes the agent's `connectors[]` ids to the same `gate()` call as its runtime and the answer rides on `spec.plugins.connectors`. On a LOCAL open (`anthropicApiRuntime`), each ready Streamable HTTP connector is opened by the app's `ConnectorOpener` — `openMcpConnector` of `@agentic/mcp`, injected in `apps/web/src/plugins/catalogue.ts` so `packages/platform` does not depend on `@agentic/mcp` — with its credentials opened through `Registry.openSecret(name, connectorPluginId)` (the connector plugin's own grants, audited) and a 10 s deadline for connect + `tools/list`; its tools join the roster as `<namespace>__<tool>` (`connectorToolPrefix`) and it is closed with the session. Nothing about a connector fails the session: missing, turned off, stdio, a secret not set, a name clash or an unreachable server leaves it out, and the system prompt's "Connectors not available" section names it and why. A change in what an open found is written with `setConnectorStatus` (status, discovered tool names), never on every open; credential values are scrubbed from any recorded error. Approval: a connector tool goes through the agent's rules, then its `tools[]` grant by the namespaced name, then allow — and its request is presented as `source: 'mcp'` with a category from its MCP hints (`destructiveHint` → `destructive`, `readOnlyHint` → `read`, otherwise `network`), so category rules reach it. **Connectors on a daemon-hosted session (#280, as built):** for a daemon runtime, `Routing` puts the gate's ready connectors on `OpenSpec.connectors` (`daemonConnectors`: `{id, transport, url | command/args/cwd, auth}`, secret NAMES only). A stdio connector bound to another machine (`machine`, `*` = any) is left out and, with every missing or turned-off one, named in the prompt's "Connectors not available". The spec holds no value because the Machine keeps it in durable state and re-sends it after a reconnect (EXE-09). While it opens the session, the daemon asks for the values over its own `tool.call` (`CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS_TOOL`; never offered to the model). `createToolCallPort` answers only for a ready connector the calling session's recorded gate names, opening each secret with `Registry.openSecret(name, connectorPluginId)` as the workspace owner, which is audited. An unset secret is refused and recorded on the connector's status once; nothing else is written. On the machine, `claudeCodeDriver({ connectors })` opens each connector: Streamable HTTP with `openMcpConnector`; stdio with `openStdioMcpConnector` (`@agentic/mcp/node`), which spawns the server in the connector's `cwd` or the session's (inside the environment's `cwdRoots`, else left out) with the stdio client's allowlisted environment plus its credential variables. Both share a 10 s deadline. The tools join the session's client tools on Claude Code's tool server as `<id>__<tool>`. The daemon's compiled policy sees them as `source: 'mcp'` with a category from the hints (`withConnectorPolicy`, the same mapping as `connectorPolicy`), and the connectors are closed with the session. A connector that cannot be opened is left out, named in the prompt and in the capability report (`connector:<id>`), with credential values scrubbed from the reason; the session still opens. The values are held in memory for that session only, never logged or written by the daemon. **Gap:** a daemon open does not record the connector's status or discovered tools; only an unset secret is recorded. `capabilityReport()` and the manifest's `unsupported:<op>` capabilities enumerate what the client leaves out — resources, prompts, sampling, elicitation, roots, logging, completions, tasks, `list_changed`, progress, the GET stream, resumption, OAuth discovery — and which of those a given server actually offers (PLG-09). Server-initiated requests are answered `-32601`, never dropped.
- **Platform MCP server = orchestration surface** (`/_agentic/mcp`, `createMcpToolHandler`). Design constraint from day one: every platform capability is an actor method, and both the web UI and the MCP server are thin projections over the same methods, so an external MCP client (Claude Code on a laptop, Claude Desktop, any MCP- or A2A-speaking orchestrator) can drive every daemon on every machine. Tool families, annotated readOnly/destructive so `allowReadOnly` composes: `machines.list`, `environments.list/doctor`, `agents.list/get`, `sessions.open(agentId, environmentId, cwd)`, `sessions.prompt`, `sessions.respond`, `sessions.cancel`, `sessions.tail(from)`, `tasks.create/delegate/get/tree/cancel`, `chats.post/history`, `memory.search/remember`, `schedules.create`. Machine selection is explicit in every call (EXE-12). Shipped (#50, `packages/mcp/src/server` + `packages/platform/src/auth/oauth-server` + `apps/web/src/auth/oauth-server`): wire names are `<family>_<op>` (`sessions_open`, …; `defineTool` names cannot carry a dot), the family IS the OAuth scope that gates it (`hasScope`, refused as an `isError` tool result naming the scope), and every tool runs over a per-principal `PlatformPort` — in the app a fresh `actor()` call per capability under the external principal, so each actor's own `authorize` chain decides; only the Workspace index read and `Routing.run` execute as the workspace's user (platform machinery, as the schedule trigger does). `sessions_open(agentId, machineId, environmentId, cwd?)` = Task.create with `origin { kind: 'external', clientId }` + `Routing.run`, after checking the named machine reports the named environment (404 otherwise, never a silent switch) and that `cwd` lies within its `cwdRoots` by `pathWithin` on the machine's OS (400 otherwise — a sibling prefix such as `C:/src2` for root `C:/src` included); the `cwd` becomes the task's `workdir`, so the session runs in it (`OpenSpec.cwd`, #190); `sessions_tail(sessionId, from?, limit?)` is a bounded page over `Session.events` with a `next` cursor. `tasks_delegate(taskId, agentId, objective, …, callId?)` = `Task.delegate` on the parent under the external principal (limits, depth, parent parked `waiting {child}`, COL-03) + `Routing.run` on the child as the driver, returning the child for `tasks_get` / `tasks_tree` to follow; `environments_doctor(machineId, environmentId?)` = `Machine.doctor` (#43). `chats_file_get(chatId, fileId)` (#209, `chats` scope) = `Chat.fileAccess` under the external principal through the optional `PlatformPort.chats.fileAccess` (missing and not visible are one `not found` error), then the bytes from the optional `files: ChatFileStore` of `createPlatformMcpHandler` — the same store instance the actors get (#207): text for a text-like type (cut at `CHAT_FILE_TEXT_MAX_BYTES`, `truncated`), an MCP `image` block for a `MODEL_IMAGE_TYPES` type, the record otherwise or without a store (`file bytes unavailable on this host`); `chats_history` keeps chat-file parts as their `agentic-file:` URIs. `usage_limits(machineId?, runtime?)` (#272, `usage` scope, read-only): every account (a machine's environment) with its latest `QuotaSnapshot` and `ageMs`, and `null` before the first report. Both surfaces return the same `UsageLimits` shape (`usageLimitsOf`, `packages/platform/src/machine/usage.ts`):
  - **MCP:** `PlatformPort.usage.limits`, bound in `apps/web`. The tool gate checks `usage`, then the machines are read as the workspace's user, so a client granted `usage` alone sees limits without `machines`.
  - **Agents:** the platform tool `usage_limits` (`PlatformPorts.usage`, granted by `ToolGrant` like the others). `createActorToolPorts({ machines })` reads the index as the workspace's user and each Machine under the agent's own principal.
- **Projects on the MCP surface (#334):** `PlatformPort.projects { list, setChatProject }` beside `schedules`. `projects_list` (scope `projects`, read-only) = `Workspace.projects` read as the workspace's user (owner-only, like the machine index), mapped to `ProjectSummary { id, name, description?, machines, environments }` (`projectFolderPlaces`, #702) — the machines with a folder and the environments with one of their own, never the folders themselves. `chats_set_project(chatId, projectId | null)` (scope **`chats`**, write) = `Chat.setProject` under the external principal: the chat is what changes, and `scopeOfTool` takes the family before the first `_` as the gating scope, so the tool carries the `chats_` prefix rather than the issue's `chat_set_project` — a client granted `chats` alone may set it, one granted `projects` alone may only list. The Chat's 400 (unknown project) and 403 surface as `isError` results with the message. The agent-side counterpart is the `projects` platform tool (§7).

  The description tells the model to weigh `resetsAt` and `ageMs`. Nothing is switched automatically (EXE-12). Declared gaps (`PLATFORM_MCP_UNSUPPORTED`): MCP resources and prompts. OAuth grant/revoke events are not on the Audit catalogue yet (`AUDIT_KINDS` has no `oauth.*` kind) — a follow-up once the catalogue grows.
  - **External-client OAuth (RFC 8414 / 7591 / 9728, OAuth 2.1).** `createOAuthServer({ secret, issuer, resource, store })` is pure `Request → Response` over WebCrypto and an `OAuthStore` (`memoryOAuthStore` in tests, `actorOAuthStore` over the global `OAuthClients` directory — anonymous `register`/`get`, like the `PairingDirectory` — and the per-workspace `OAuthGrants` actor `{ws}:oauth:grants`, owner-only). Public clients only (`token_endpoint_auth_method: none`), PKCE S256 required, redirect URI exact-match (loopback port excepted, RFC 8252), scopes = core's `Scope` union (`ALL_SCOPES`). Consent is a minimal HTML form rendered only for a signed-in user (else 302 to `/auth/login?returnTo=…`), sealed as an `oatx` transaction bound to that user; the user may untick scopes — the grant is the ticked subset. Tokens are HMAC-sealed on the package's `seal` envelope: `oacd` code (10 min, single use through the store), `oaac` access (1 h; decodes to `{ kind: 'external', workspaceId, clientId, scopes }`, `verify` also checks the grant is not revoked), `oarf` refresh (30 d, rotates on every use; a replayed generation revokes the grant), RFC 7009 revocation. The 401 on `/_agentic/mcp` carries `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer resource_metadata="…/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource/_agentic/mcp"`, which is all an MCP client needs: **`claude mcp add --transport http agentic https://<origin>/_agentic/mcp`** → the first request is challenged → the client reads the protected-resource metadata → the authorization-server metadata → registers itself at `/oauth/register` → opens the browser at `/oauth/authorize` (login, consent) → exchanges the code at `/oauth/token` → calls tools with the bearer, refreshing silently; no token is ever pasted. The MCP SDK asks for every scope the resource advertises, so narrowing happens on the consent screen.
- A2A 1.0, JSON-RPC binding only (`@agentic/a2a`, #31): `createA2aHandler({port})` is a fetch handler over a `SessionPort` (`agents()` = the agents flagged `exposeA2A`, `session(agentId, contextId, request)` = the live session behind a context, optional `authorize`) and a `TaskStore` for snapshots — no actors in the package. A card per exposed agent at `{base}/{agentId}/.well-known/agent-card.json` (the single/default agent also at the root well-known path); SendMessage, SendStreamingMessage (SSE), GetTask, ListTasks, CancelTask. Mapping: contextId = session, task = turn (task id = turn id), text/reasoning parts = artifact chunks, `request` = INPUT_REQUIRED answered by a message on the task, other events (tool calls, usage, errors, the turn result) = data parts under the `agentic` extension (`required: false`, a plain peer sees text only). State mapping: SUBMITTED = queued, WORKING = active, INPUT_REQUIRED / AUTH_REQUIRED = waiting, terminals 1:1 (`turn-end` stop reasons: end_turn/max_* → COMPLETED, error → FAILED, cancelled → CANCELED, refusal → REJECTED). Unsupported and declared: gRPC/REST bindings, push-notification config (`-32003`), extended card and Subscribe (`-32004`) — JSON-RPC errors, never a 500. Client `a2aAgent(cardUrl | card, {fetch, auth})` implements `Agent` (capabilities `resume: false, cancel: true, tools: 'none'`, `promptParts` from `defaultInputModes`, `permissions: 'harness-filtered'` when the card declares the agentic extension else `'none'`; `agent.a2a.unsupported` lists what is not translated, PLG-09), streams or polls GetTask, and passes the conformance subset (AC-14). The full event table lives in `packages/a2a/README.md`. **A2A peers as runtimes (#246):** a remote agent a user adds on /plugins ("Add A2A peer") is a `kind: runtime` plugin minted by `a2aPeer({ id, cardUrl })` — id `a2a.<id>` (a dot, not a colon: Registry ids and secret names share `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, and the id is a `/plugins/:id` segment), config `{ cardUrl }`, an optional bearer secret `a2a-<id>-token`, permissions `network:<host>` + `secret:a2a-<id>-token` — registered on and granted what it declares (the token is set first, so a refused one leaves nothing half-added). The runtime catalogue resolves the prefix: `withInstanceRuntimes(fixed, { 'a2a.': (id) => a2aPeerRuntime(id) })` (`packages/platform/src/routing/factory.ts`) keeps the build's own runtimes first, then makes a local `RuntimeImpl` per peer id; each open reads the card URL from the gate's plugin config, opens the token through `Registry.openSecret` (never kept) and opens an `a2aAgent` session, continuing the peer's context from the session ref's `contextId`. Whether the id may run is the router's `gate()` as for any runtime: no such plugin or turned off fails the task `plugin-disabled`. The agent form offers it with the other runtime plugins (#234). No approval policy is handed to the peer session: a plain peer asks nothing, and an agentic one's requests surface as the Session's requests.
  - **Mounted as a plugin (#245, `apps/web/src/a2a`).** The `agentic.a2a.server` plugin (`a2aServerPlugin`, kind `a2a`, in the catalogue `enabledByDefault: false`, config `{ exposedAgents: string[] }` — agent ids or names, matched without case) is the switch; `createA2aMount({ actors })` in `entry.cloudflare.ts` answers `/.well-known/agent-card.json` and `/_agentic/a2a/{agentId}[/.well-known/agent-card.json]` with `createA2aHandler`, one handler per workspace. Every request: a bearer token of the platform's OAuth server (the MCP clients' — same issuer, store and grants; the mount builds its own `createOAuthServer` over them) or 401 with the resource-metadata challenge → the plugin enabled in the token's workspace or **404**, the same answer as nothing mounted → the `tasks` + `sessions` scopes or 403 `insufficient_scope`. `agents()` reads the plugin and the agents as the workspace (exposure is the plugin's decision, not the client's scope); a card is `promptParts: 'text'`. A context is `platformA2aSession` (`a2a/session.ts`) keyed by client + agent + contextId: each A2A task is ONE platform task — `Task.create` as the client (`origin { kind: 'external', clientId }`) + `Routing.run` as the workspace through `createActorPlatformPort`, so the runtime gate, the approval policy and the audit apply — whose session's first turn is followed through `sessions.tail` (polled, 250 ms) and handed back as the A2A turn; a task that fails before a session opens is `turn-start` + `turn-end {error}` naming the platform code (`plugin-disabled`, `no-api-key`, …), i.e. a FAILED A2A task. `respond` answers the session's request, `cancel` cancels the task. Earlier exchanges of the context go into the next task's `context`. Limits, v1: live A2A task state and context history are per isolate (the handler's in-memory `TaskStore`) — a `GetTask`, or the answer to INPUT_REQUIRED, that lands on another isolate does not find the task; the platform task itself is durable. The card carries no `securitySchemes` yet.
- Principals: `{kind: 'user', userId, workspaceId}` (GitHub OAuth behind an `AuthProvider` interface → `__Host-session` cookie → `createServerApp({authenticate})`), `{kind: 'machine', workspaceId, machineId}` (pairing code: 6 chars, 10 min, single use → machine token, hash stored), `{kind: 'agent', workspaceId, agentId, sessionId, taskId}` (minted per session for tool callbacks), `{kind: 'external', clientId, scopes[]}` (OAuth 2.1 + Dynamic Client Registration, RFC 8414/7591/9728, so `claude mcp add --transport http agentic <url>` needs no manual token; scopes gate tool families). Every actor's `authorize` chain starts with `sameWorkspace`. Principal propagates unchanged across `ctx.actor()` hops; `Task.delegate` re-mints an agent principal for the child (COL-10).
  - Auth seams (#32, `packages/platform/src/auth`, bound in `apps/web/src/auth`): `serverAuth({ sessionSecret, machines })` → `{ authenticate, codec }` for `createServerApp<Principal>`. Precedence: `Authorization: Bearer amt.<ws>.<machine>.<secret>` → machine principal (SHA-256 looked up on the Machine actor through the injected `MachineTokenLookup`; `revokedAt` → anonymous), `Bearer agt.…` → sealed agent principal, else `__Host-session` (HMAC-SHA256 seal of `{ userId, workspaceId }`, 30 days) → user principal; anything else `null`, never a throw. Login is `beginOAuth` / `completeOAuth` over the `AuthProvider` seam (`githubAuthProvider` over `fetch`; state + PKCE S256 in a sealed `__Host-oauth` transient, 10 min). In-process callers with no request (task bodies) act as a minted agent via `actor(Def, key).with({ context: asPrincipal(principal) })`. v1 identity: personal workspace, `workspaceId === userId === gh_<github id>` (colon-free so `workspaceOfKey` parses every actor key). Pairing: `issuePairing` stores only the code hash on Workspace; `POST /auth/pair { code, name }` verifies (used → expired → mismatch), consumes, and returns the one-time machine token. Stored API keys: `encryptSecret(importWorkspaceKek(WORKSPACE_KEK), value, aad)`.
  - **Elevation (#355, #478).** A security-sensitive machine change from the web — the folders the web may use, browsing outside `cwdRoots`, `bypassPermissions` on, revoking or removing a machine — needs more than the session cookie. `GET /auth/elevate?returnTo=` runs the same OAuth flow with `purpose: 'elevate'` in the transient (a signed-in user only); the callback compares the identity that comes back with the session's user and, on a match, sets `__Host-elevated` (`sealElevation`, `elv`, `ELEVATION_TTL_MS` = 10 min; `Secure; Path=/; HttpOnly`, no `Domain`) beside the session — never re-minting it; another identity is 403 `user_mismatch`, no session 401. `authenticateRequest` reads the cookie beside `__Host-session` and sets `elevatedUntil` on the user principal only when it names the same user, so an elevation cannot be lent to another session; `principalCodec` carries the field across hops (a non-finite value never decodes). Owner-only actor methods call `requireElevated(ctx.principal, now)` → 403 `elevation-required: …` (core's `ELEVATION_REQUIRED`). The dev login (`/auth/dev-login`, `elevate=1` / `elevate: true` / the form's checkbox) mints the cookie without GitHub, for previews and the workers tests. The page half (`apps/web/src/pages/machines/{elevate.ts,ElevateDialog.tsx}`): a refused call opens "Confirm with GitHub to continue", Continue keeps the change per machine in `sessionStorage` (ten minutes) and goes to `/auth/elevate`, and back on the page the dialog reopens in its resume shape — one Confirm click sends it, nothing runs on its own. Revoke and Remove go through it, so do the folders card's Save and Browse… and an environment save that turns bypass on (#482); a Browse… that needed elevation reopens the browser on the way back with no confirm, since it changes nothing.
- Credentials: Claude Code OAuth stays on the machine under `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (EXE-10); the platform stores only `authStatus`. API keys encrypted with `WORKSPACE_KEK` (AES-GCM via WebCrypto). Retention (OPS-10, `docs/retention.md`): workspace settings `{sessionLogDays, artifactDays}`; `exportAll` writes one NDJSON file per actor kind plus `manifest.json` through the `ArtifactSink` port (R2 in `apps/web`), every row read through the owning actor's `get` / `export` as the owner (a task body inherits no principal, so the cascade calls `actor(Def, key).with({ context: asPrincipal(owner) })`), secrets by name only; `deleteAll` purges every child record the index implies (agents, their memory scopes, chats + pages, schedules, inbox, registry) plus whatever `WorkspaceStore.list` returns (tasks, sessions), then `clearState()`s itself (`apps/web` purges each record inside its own Durable Object via `POST /_agentic/purge`, `SESSION_SECRET`-authenticated, and has no `list`, #100) — tested to leave no record in storage; copies handed to runtimes, providers and peers are listed as outside platform control. Plugin lifecycle (AC-13): `Registry.disable` returns `dependents(id)` and `requireEnabled(ctx, ws, id)` refuses NEW use from then on; running work is never interrupted by it.

### Target — plugins track (#224)

What the bullets above describe is built but not load-bearing: nothing in production registers a plugin, calls `requireEnabled`, reads `PluginState.config` or opens a Registry secret (`docs/runbook.md` §10). This is the design the sub-issues of #224 implement (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-19); each replaces its part of this subsection with the as-built text.

- **As wired in the app (#231).** `apps/web/src/plugins/catalogue.ts` exports `pluginCatalogue` (the manifests, memory default before flat so it is the slot's default) and `runtimeCatalogue(options)` (`anthropic-api` → `anthropicApiRuntime`, the harness runtimes `claude-code`, `copilot-cli`, `codex-cli` → daemon, #322); `platformActors` hands the first to `defineRegistry({ kek, catalogue })` and the second to both `createSessionFactory({ registry, runtimes })` and `defineRoutingActor({ registry, runtimes })` (overridable as `PlatformPorts.catalogue` / `runtimes`). The Worker has no `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: the only key is the workspace's `anthropic-api-key` secret. Memory and learning follow the active plugin (`memoryCatalogue` / `learningCatalogue`, #242; the flat memory plugin durable on the FlatMemory actor, #281); notification implementations are still wired statically (#244). The A2A server (#245) is `{ manifest: a2aServerPlugin, enabledByDefault: false }`; its implementation is the Worker's A2A mount, which asks the Registry on every request (see the A2A bullet above).
- **Catalogue = manifest + implementation, assembled at the composition root.** Manifests live beside their code (`@agentic/runtimes`: `anthropic-api`, `claude-code`, `copilot-cli`, `codex-cli`; `@agentic/memory`; `@agentic/learning`; `notify`: web push; `mcpConnector()` and an A2A peer template mint one manifest per instance a user adds). `apps/web/src/actors.app.ts` builds `{ manifest, impl }[]` and injects it: `defineRegistry({ kek, catalogue })` gets the manifests; Session, Routing and Inbox get the implementations — runtime `{ host: 'local', open } | { host: 'daemon' }` (replaces `if (runtime !== 'anthropic-api')` in `createSessionFactory`), memory → `MemoryPlugin`, learning → `LearningPluginFactory`, notification → `NotificationChannel`. `packages/platform` never imports a plugin package it does not already depend on.
- **Manifest.** `config` becomes `ConfigSchema`, a typed JSON-Schema subset with a pure `validateConfig` / `configDefaults` in core; `secrets[]` is declared apart from it, each entry with its `secret:<name>` scope. `pluginReadiness(state, facts)` — `ready | disabled | needs-config | needs-secret | needs-grant | needs-sign-in | needs-machine | no-kek` — is one pure core function the pages and the platform share; the caller supplies the facts (secret names, environments, whether a KEK is set, which plugins are signed out), the function hops nowhere. `needs-sign-in` (#627, OPS-04) comes after the grants and before `needs-machine`, with `missing: []`. A manifest may declare `tools[]` (`PluginToolDeclaration`: the namespaced name sessions see, e.g. `gmail__send-email`, plus an optional `defaultMode: ToolMode`); `defaultToolMode(annotations)` derives one from MCP-style hints — read-only allows, an explicit `destructiveHint: false` allows, anything else asks — MCP defaults `destructiveHint` to true for a tool that is not read-only (#672, PLG-09).
- **Built-ins are virtual until touched — as built (#229).** `defineRegistry({ kek, catalogue })`; an entry is a manifest or `{ manifest, enabledByDefault: false }` for one that ships switched off. `current(id) = stored record ?? catalogue default` (enabled, every declared scope granted, `registeredAt: 0`); state keeps the owner's config values only and every view merges them over `configDefaults`, so a new build's defaults flow through. Reads never write (pinned by a test on the storage's saves); the first mutation materialises the record and saves in the turn; a stored built-in's manifest is always overlaid from the build — undeclared grants lapse, a scope no build declared before is granted once (`seenScopes`), one the owner revoked stays revoked; `register` over and `remove` of a built-in refuse `builtin`; a plugin a later build drops falls back to its stored record. `openSecret` works for a built-in that was never materialised. `memory` and `learning` are single-slot kinds: `active[kind]` names the one plugin in use.
- **Secrets are never config — as built (#229).** In through `Registry.setSecret`, out only through `openSecret(name, pluginId)` (enabled + granted, audited). `configure` (and `register`'s config) validates against the manifest's `ConfigSchema` with defaults filled in — `BadConfigError` names every path, unknown keys included, and nothing is stored. A manifest's `secrets[]` entry without its `secret:` permission is a `bad-manifest`.
- **One hop.** The Registry side is built (#229): `gate({ runtime })` → `{ runtime: { id, enabled, config } | null, memory, learning, channels: { id, config }[] }` — `null` when no runtime plugin has that id, memory / learning the ACTIVE plugin of the slot, channels the enabled notification plugins, every `config` ready to use; it reports and never throws. `overview()` is one live read (plugins, active slots, secret names, `hasKek` — the `pluginReadiness` facts minus the machines) and `dependentsAll()` one walk of the workspace for every plugin. Still to come (#230): `Routing.run` calls `Registry.gate({ runtime })` once, before a route is written: `{ runtime: { enabled, config }, memory, learning, channels }`. The result rides on the `SessionOpenSpec`, so the session's memory and learning ports need no second call. Pages read `overview()` and `dependentsAll()` — one call each, not one per plugin.
- **Notification prefs (#302, as built).** `defineInbox({ workspace })` reads `WorkspaceSettings.notifications` over one `Workspace.get()` hop as the owner, first thing in `Inbox.push` / `Inbox.append`, in the same turn as the `gate().channels` hop. `push: false` (the default) skips every outbound channel — static and notification plugin — without asking the Registry and records no attempt: the user's choice, not a failure. Prefs that cannot be read push nothing and record a failed `PREFS_CHANNEL` (`settings`) attempt (OPS-04). `inbox: false` still records the notification, flagged `muted`: Needs you and a chat's open questions read the list, `unread()` skips it (`docs/decisions.md`, 2026-09-25). No `workspace` wired (tests, the default `Inbox`) means both on.
- **Flow: a key set in the UI reaches a turn.** `/plugins/anthropic-api` → `Registry.setSecret('anthropic-api-key', …)` (sealed under `WORKSPACE_KEK`) → a message → `Routing.run` → `gate()` (applies `config.defaultModel` when the agent names none) → the session factory's local runtime → `Registry.openSecret('anthropic-api-key', 'anthropic-api')` → `createPlatformModelAgent`. No key → `no-api-key`, naming the plugin page. There is no deployment-wide key, and no plaintext is cached in the isolate.
- **Flow: a plugin is disabled.** `Registry.disable` saves, audits and returns the dependents → the next `Routing.run` fails the task `plugin-disabled` (recoverable); schedules follow, since a firing goes through `Routing.run`; the `fallback-api` path is refused; `openSecret` refuses as defence in depth; running sessions finish. Memory or learning disabled → new sessions skip retrieval and writes, the data is kept. Re-enabling restores everything.
- **Flow: an environment is added from the web.** Machine page → owner-only `Machine.putEnvironment(input)` (never a platform or MCP tool; elevated when it turns `bypassPermissions` on) → `env.request` frame, tracked like `fsRequest` → the daemon checks its `policy.json` (set from the page through `policy.request`, or on the machine; locked there wins), contains every root by realpath, allocates the `profileDir` itself, writes `environments.json` atomically, calls `setEnvironments` → `env` + `env.response` → the Machine folds the descriptors and the page shows the row, `signed-out`, with **Sign in…** (the runtime's login relayed, #484) or `agentic-daemon env login <id>` for a runtime the daemon cannot relay.

### Target — connectors that sign in (#536)

Decided 2026-09-23 (`docs/decisions.md`). This is the design the sub-issues of #536 implement; each replaces its bullet with the as-built text (#530–#534 are built).

- **Record (#530, as built).** `ConnectorTransport` has `'conduit'`. A conduit `ConnectorRecord` / `GateConnector` carries `connector` (the conduit connector id, `gmail`) and, once the owner has connected, `account` (the conduit account id); `Registry.putConnector` refuses a conduit record with a `url`, `command`, `secrets` or `auth`, so it never names a credential. `ConnectorOpenInput` is a union on `kind`: `mcp` (credential values opened for this session) or `conduit` (`{ id, pluginId, connector, account }`, ids only). `openSessionConnectors` leaves a conduit connector without an account out as "not connected yet (/plugins/<id>)", hands a connected one to the opener with the session's `ConnectorOpenContext` (#533: `{ workspaceId, principal, secret(name, pluginId) }` — the session's agent principal, and the connector plugin's secrets through `Registry.openSecret` as the owner, as `createSessionFactory` opens them), records what the open found with `setConnectorStatus`, and never fails the session over it. On a daemon-hosted session a conduit connector still runs on the Worker (#534, below).
- **Engine and tools (#531, `@agentic/connectors`, as built).** Edge-safe over `@agentic/core`, `@aigntiq/conduit` (root entry only) and `@aigntiq/conduit-connectors`. `createConnectorEngine({ secret, accounts, transient, locks, clients, redirectUri, http? })` is `createConduit` with the ports injected; it checks the secret (≥ 32 characters) and the redirect URI (absolute http(s)) at construction. `conduitTools(engine, { id, connector, account, owner })` returns the `OpenedConnector` shape: one tool per `action` / `search` operation named `<id>__<operation>`, its input schema the spec's minus the `x-` UI hints, hints from the spec (`destructive: true` → destructive, a `search` or an `action` whose request and steps use only `GET` / `HEAD` → read-only, anything else → no hint, which `connectorPolicy` rules as `network`). A failed call throws `ConnectorToolError`; a `needsReauth` account gives `code: 'needs_reauth'` and asks the agent to have the owner reconnect. `clientFromSecrets(openSecret, pluginId)` resolves the OAuth client per lookup from the plugin's own `<pluginId>-client-id` / `<pluginId>-client-secret` (#548: Registry secret names are workspace-wide, so a second conduit connector never reads Gmail's client). `gmailConnectorPlugin` declares both (`gmail-client-id`, `gmail-client-secret`), plus `connector-engine-secret` (not required: the platform generates it), the network hosts and `tools:gmail`; its capabilities list `operation:<id>` per tool and `unsupported:trigger:new-email`.
- **Accounts (#532, as built).** The `ConnectorAccounts` actor (`{ws}:connector-accounts`, served by the one `ActorHost` DO — no binding) implements conduit's `AccountStore` (compare-and-set on `version`), `TransientStore` (OAuth state and PKCE verifiers, TTL, read-and-delete, at most 1000 in flight) and a lease `LockProvider` (`lock` / `unlock`, interleaved, in memory; CAS makes a lost lease harmless). It holds conduit's sealed credential string only and refuses one that is plain JSON. Every port method is `ownerOrAgent` (the owner on the routes, a session's agent principal refreshing mid-call); a machine or external client reads only `accounts()` / `exportRows()`, which carry no credentials. `connector.connected` / `connector.needs-reauth` / `connector.disconnected` are audited (History's "Connected accounts" filter). `connectorAccountStores(client)` adapts an actor client to the three ports. The Workspace cascade exports it (`connector-accounts.ndjson`: id, connector, status, address) and purges it with the workspace. Every mutation ends in `ctx.save()`.
- **Routes and UI (#533, as built; `apps/web/src/connectors`).** The engine is built per request (`workspaceConnectorEngine`) over the workspace's `ConnectorAccounts` as the caller, `clientFromSecrets` over the plugin's secrets, and `connector-engine-secret` — one per workspace, shared by every conduit connector plugin (they share the one `ConnectorAccounts` store it keys) — a random 32-byte value generated on the first Connect (`ensureEngineSecret`, owner-only `setSecret`) and kept as a Registry secret, so it keys the credential cipher per workspace. `redirectUri` is `/_agentic/connectors/callback` on the origin the request came in on — the one the page shows — so the provider returns the owner to the host that holds their session cookie, even on a deployment with several hostnames. The Worker mounts, beside the auth and file routes, for the build's conduit connectors (`conduitConnectorCatalogue`: `gmail`), the signed-in owner's only:
  - `GET /_agentic/connectors/:id/start` — the plugin must be on; writes the conduit record if missing; `auth.begin` with `owner = workspace`, `returnTo = /plugins/:id`, and the record's existing account (a Reconnect keeps its id); redirects to the provider.
  - `GET /_agentic/connectors/callback` — `auth.complete` (conduit verifies the signed state and the single-use handshake, exchanges the code with PKCE, seals the tokens), writes the account id onto the record, redirects to the verified same-site `returnTo` with `?connected=1`. Any failure lands on the plugin page with `?connect_error=`.
  - `POST /_agentic/connectors/:id/disconnect` — same-origin only; `auth.revoke` (best effort at the provider, the account deleted either way) and the record kept without its account.

  There is no HTTP `execute`: operations run only as session tools. The opener handed to `anthropicApiRuntime` (`connectorOpener` in `src/plugins/catalogue.ts`) dispatches `mcp` → `openMcpConnector` and `conduit` → `conduitTools` over an engine built at open from the session's `ConnectorOpenContext`: its stores as the session's agent principal (a refresh inside a tool call writes as the session), the plugin's secrets as the owner. Gmail ships in the catalogue off (`enabledByDefault: false`). Its `/plugins/gmail` page is the plugin page plus a sign-in panel (`ConduitConnect.tsx`): the client id and secret as the plugin's secret fields (no field for the engine secret), the redirect URI with Copy, the account's status from `ConnectorAccounts.accounts()` (connected as whom, or NEEDS RECONNECTING), Connect / Reconnect / Disconnect, the operations agents get (AGT-09) and the "Testing" consent screen's 7-day refresh tokens. Setup steps: `docs/runbook.md` "Connect Gmail".
- **Daemon-hosted sessions (#534, as built).** A conduit connector never opens on a machine; its tools do. Two more calls ride the daemon's own `tool.call`, beside `CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS_TOOL` (#280), with their shapes in `@agentic/core` (`daemon.ts`):
  - `CONNECTOR_TOOLS_TOOL` (`connector_tools`, input `{}`): the daemon asks once while it opens a session (`openDaemonConnectors`, every harness driver), never offered to the model. The platform (`createToolCallPort` → `platformConnectorTools`) reads the calling session's recorded gate, opens every ready, connected conduit connector through the app's `ConnectorOpener` with the same `ConnectorOpenContext` a local session gets (the session's agent principal; the plugin's secrets through `Registry.openSecret` as the owner), records what the open found with `setConnectorStatus`, and answers `PlatformConnectorTools`: per connector, `ConnectorToolDeclaration`s — the namespaced name, description, input JSON Schema and read/destructive hints — plus the ones it could not open, with why. No token, OAuth client, engine secret or account id is in it.
  - `CONNECTOR_CALL_TOOL` (`connector_call`, input `{ connectorId, tool, input }`): each declaration is served on the machine as a client tool beside the platform's (a clash with a platform tool's name leaves the connector out), and its `execute` is this call. The platform (`callPlatformConnector`) runs it only for a connector the calling session's gate names as ready, conduit and connected (`forbidden` otherwise), opens it through the same opener and context — so a refresh inside the call writes as the session and `needsReauth` behaves as on a local session — runs the tool, and returns its output. A failure is a `tool.result` error: a `ConnectorToolError` keeps its code (`needs_reauth`) and its message for the agent; anything else is `internal` with every secret opened for the call blanked.

  Approval is the machine's, as for an MCP connector there: the declared hints join the session's connector annotations, so `withConnectorPolicy` presents each call to the daemon-compiled rules as `source: 'mcp'` with `connectorCategory`'s category (a read allowed, sending — `network` — asked or denied as the rules say) before any `tool.call` leaves the machine. `daemonConnectors` puts nothing of a conduit connector on `OpenSpec` (the Machine keeps the spec, so it never names an account either); an unconnected one is still named in the prompt with why. The Worker wires it in `platformActors` (`createToolCallPort({ connectors: connectorOpener(...) })`, the opener `anthropic-api` sessions use). Why a `tool.call` pair and not a spec field: no `OpenSpec` or daemon-protocol schema changes, so an older daemon never asks and an older platform answers `unsupported` — the session simply has no conduit tools — and the declarations are computed on the Worker, from the connector spec it already holds, at every open. The cost is one round trip per open, and one Registry hop (an audited `secret.opened` of the engine secret) per call.
- **Incoming Gmail (#535, as built: polling).** The trigger is a Schedule entry with `source: { kind: 'connector', connector: <plugin id>, query? }` and an `agentId` (the hook: §4 Schedule, "Watching a source"). `/plugins/gmail` sets it up (`pages/plugins/ConnectorTrigger.tsx`: the agent, a Gmail search filter — default `in:inbox` —, an interval of 5/10/15/30/60 minutes as a UTC cron, an optional prompt, on/off), and /schedules lists it too. The app's `TriggerPort` is `connectorTrigger` (`apps/web/src/connectors/trigger.ts`) in front of `scheduleTrigger`: a firing with a connector source runs one poll as the workspace owner — the Registry, the plugin's secrets and the `ConnectorAccounts` through the same `workspaceConnectorEngine` the sign-in routes build.
  - Plugin off → nothing is read, the entry stays on. No account on the record, the account gone, no engine secret, or an account whose sign-in expired (`needsReauth`, seen before the poll or by it) → the firing answers `{ pause: reason }` and pushes one `reminder` notification with `ref { kind: 'schedule' }`; the entry turns itself off (no crash loop against Google) until the owner switches it back on.
  - Otherwise `pollGmail` (`@agentic/connectors`, `src/triggers/gmail.ts`). conduit's Gmail spec declares a `new-email` poll trigger, but conduit 0.1 runs no triggers and the spec has no history operation, so a poll is `search-messages` for `(<query>) after:<since − 600 s>` (the overlap covers Gmail's search-index lag; the first poll looks back 15 minutes) minus the delivered ids the cursor keeps (≤ 500), oldest first, at most 20 per poll (a capped poll keeps its window), then one `get-message` each for From / Subject / snippet.
  - Each arrival is ONE task: id `triggeredTaskId(scheduleId, messageId)` (deterministic; `Task.create` is idempotent), `origin { kind: 'trigger', triggerId: scheduleId }`, the entry's agent as assignee and owner, the entry's prompt as objective (a default otherwise), the headers and the `<id>__get-message` hint as context. A task that lands `queued` goes to `Routing.run` as the owner, fire and forget. The poll's new cursor is the firing's `{ cursor }`, saved by the Schedule in the same turn, so neither a re-poll nor a retried firing starts a second turn. A transient failure throws; the Schedule's retry/drop applies with the cursor unmoved.
  - The manifest lists the trigger as `trigger:new-email` (no longer `unsupported:`). The agent reads the mail through its own Gmail grant under its own policy: a task carries no narrower tool grant (`TaskContract` has none).
  - Not built: Pub/Sub push (Gmail `watch` → a public Worker route, the watch renewed by a Schedule) — a follow-up.

### Target — plugins redesign (#625)

Decided 2026-09-24 (`docs/decisions.md`, "plugins redesign"). This is the design the sub-issues of #625 implement; each replaces its bullet with the as-built text. The pages are specified in `docs/design/plugins/HANDOFF-plugins.md`; their routes are in §10.

- **Tool declarations (#627).** `PluginManifest.tools?: readonly PluginToolDeclaration[]`, each `{ name, title?, description?, defaultMode?: ToolMode }` with `ToolMode = 'allow' | 'ask' | 'deny'` (core's `agent.ts`; `ToolGrant.mode` takes the same values). `name` is the namespaced tool name a session sees (`gmail__send-email`, `connectorToolPrefix`). A plugin with a fixed tool set declares it on the manifest (conduit connectors, from their operations, #632); an MCP connector reports its tools on its connector record when an open discovers them (#633). `defaultToolMode(annotations)` gives a tool with no declared mode its default from the hints: read-only → allow, explicit `destructiveHint: false` → allow, anything else (unannotated included, the MCP default) → ask (#672). `assertPluginManifest` checks the names are unique and each `defaultMode` is a `ToolMode`.
- **Workspace tool policy (#631).** The Registry record carries `toolPolicy?: Record<string, ToolMode>`, set through owner-only `setToolPolicy(id, tool, mode)` (a tool neither declared nor reported is `unknown-tool`; saved in the turn; audited `plugin.tool-policy`). The effective mode of a tool is `stored ?? declared defaultMode ?? 'allow'` (`toolPolicy(id)`). `gate()` hands it to routing: each `GateConnector` carries the `ask` and `deny` entries only, plus `toolsGranted` (whether its `tools:<ns>` scope is covered by `grantedPermissions`). `revoke()` refuses a scope the manifest does not declare (`not-declared`, like `grant()`) and is audited `plugin.revoked`.
- **The policy chain (#636).** Every session opens under the agent's policy constrained stricter-wins by ONE constraint set: the ancestors' (`route.constraints`, a delegated task's chain) with the workspace tool policy merged in front (`withWorkspaceRules`, `packages/platform/src/routing/connectors.ts`). The workspace tool policy becomes `ApprovalRule`s, one per ask or deny entry of a usable connector (`workspaceToolRules`): `{ id: 'workspace:<plugin>:<tool>', match: { tools: [tool] }, outcome }`. They cannot simply be appended: `compilePolicy` is first-match *within* one set, and the ancestor constraints carry the parent agents' own rules, `allow` rules included (§7): appended after them, a parent's `allow gmail__send-email` would hide a workspace `deny`; prepended, a workspace `ask` would hide an ancestor `deny` (AC-12). So each workspace rule leads a block scoped to its one tool: a `deny` is the whole block; an `ask` is preceded by every ancestor rule that could match that tool, narrowed to it (`match.tools: [tool]`) with its outcome raised to at least `ask` (an ancestor `deny` keeps its id). A request for that tool always stops in its block and gets exactly the stricter of the ancestors' answer and the workspace's (tested against the two sets compiled apart and combined with `constrainPolicy`, over every mix); any other request falls through to the ancestors unchanged. An agent's own policy can only tighten the workspace default: a workspace `deny` beats an agent `allow`, and a workspace `ask` with an agent `deny` is deny. Both paths carry it in the field they already had, so there is no protocol change and no daemon version gate: `sessionPolicy({ approvalConstraints })` in the local factory, `OpenSpecPolicy.constraints` (`openSpecPolicy`) for daemon-hosted sessions, which `sessionPolicyOf` compiles the same way.
- **The `tools:` grant is enforced (#636).** A connector whose `tools:<ns>` scope is not granted (`toolsGranted === false`) is left out of a new session on both paths (`openSessionConnectors`, `daemonConnectors`), and named as unavailable with the reason "tools permission revoked"; a daemon-hosted session's `tool.call`s for it (`CONNECTOR_TOOLS_TOOL`, `CONNECTOR_CALL_TOOL`, `CONNECTOR_CREDENTIALS_TOOL`) find nothing to answer, and its ask / deny tools add no workspace rules. Revoking the scope from the plugin page removes the tools from every new session; running sessions finish (AC-13). `network:` scopes are enforced too (#642, below).
- **The `network:` grant is enforced (#642; PLG-04).** `gate()` gives each ready connector `networkHosts`: the hosts of its plugin's granted `network:<host>` scopes. Routing hands them to the app's opener as `allowedHosts` on every open (`ConnectorOpenInput`, both kinds; local sessions, `platformConnectorTools`, `callPlatformConnector`), and the opener runs the connector's fetch behind that allowlist: `openMcpConnector({ allowedHosts })` / `createMcpClient` wrap it in `guardFetch` (`@agentic/mcp`), `createConnectorEngine({ allowedHosts })` wraps conduit's `http` in `guardHttp` (`@agentic/connectors`, via `workspaceConnectorEngine` in the web opener). A request to a host not on the list never leaves: it fails with `McpNetworkError` / `ConnectorNetworkError` (code `network_not_granted`), whose message names the scope and nothing of the request, and a conduit tool call surfaces it as a `ConnectorToolError` "Permission denied: network:<host> is not granted to this connector…" (a `ConduitError`, so conduit neither retries nor wraps it). A Streamable HTTP connector whose own host is not granted is not opened, not placed on a daemon's `OpenSpec` and given no credentials; the agent is told "network permission revoked: network:<host> is not granted". A match is by `host` (with port) or `hostname`; `guardFetch` follows redirects itself (`redirect: 'manual'`) and checks every hop. conduit already does the same: it builds each request with `redirect: 'manual'`, checks every hop against the spec's hosts, and sends each one through the guarded `http` (tested). A granted `network:*` (not declarable today; `grantedNetworkHosts`) would mean no allowlist, as `scopeCovered` reads it. A gate answer without `networkHosts` (recorded before #642) has no allowlist. The Gmail poll trigger runs its engine behind the same hosts, and a refused host pauses the entry with an Inbox note naming the scope. `configure` refuses a connector `url` on a host its manifest declares no `network:` scope for (`bad-config`, "add the connector again"): such a scope can never be granted, so the connector would otherwise be stranded. Out of reach: a stdio server is a process on a machine, and daemon-side sandboxing is not done; saying so on the plugin page is left to the Granted panel (#640). The sign-in routes build their engines without the allowlist.
- **`needs-sign-in` (#627, #635).** `PluginReadinessStatus` gains `needs-sign-in`, fed by the readiness fact `PluginReadinessFacts.signedOut` (plugin ids). `pluginReadiness` checks it after the grants and before `needs-machine`, with `missing = []`. The web derives the fact from live reads: a conduit record whose account is `needsReauth`, or an MCP record whose status is an auth error (401 / 403). The pages show it as NEEDS SIGN-IN with a Sign in fix (OPS-04).
- **Installable connectors (#630).** The Add-a-connector page browses a static, typed list in the build (`apps/web/src/plugins/listings.ts`: category, transport, publisher, tools with the ones that ask by default, what it asks for, where it runs). Each entry is backed by a `pluginCatalogue` plugin or by an MCP preset URL. There is no remote index (row 9). Connecting runs the existing flows (`ConduitConnect` for conduit, the MCP probe for MCP) and grants nothing to any agent until the owner picks agents (PLG-04).

## 10. UI

Routes (`@sigx/router`): `/` (inbox + active tasks), `/chats`, `/chats/new` (#336, the daemon's deep link, before `/chats/:id`), `/chats/:id`, `/projects`, `/projects/new`, `/projects/:id` (#333), `/agents`, `/agents/:id` (overview, config + versions, memory, sessions), `/tasks`, `/tasks/:id` (tree), `/sessions/:id` (transcript), `/sessions/:id/changes`, `/sessions/:id/files` (target, #564), `/machines`, `/machines/:id`, `/schedules`, `/plugins?kind=&status=&q=`, `/plugins/connectors/add?category=&q=&selected=`, `/plugins/:id` (target, #625: the Plugins bullet below), `/settings`, `/pair`, `/history` (Audit), `/usage` (Ledger). The visual design is the handoff vendored at `docs/design/` (`HANDOFF.md` is the spec; `tokens.json`, `screenshots/`, `artboards/`). The plugins redesign (#625) has its own part at `docs/design/plugins/` (`HANDOFF-plugins.md`, the four plugin boards).

- Design system (`packages/ui/src/design-system`, `@agentic/ui/design-system`): zero-daisyui's tokens and recipes re-tuned to the handoff and compiled by `sigx zero:build ./dist/design-system.js --extra-manifest ./dist/fragment.js --out dist/ds` (the kit's `runStandardBuild`) into `dist/ds/**` — `@agentic/ui/css`, `/css/tokens`, `/register`, `/manifest.json`. One theme, `control-room` (dark only in v1, `docs/decisions.md`), is the only default: the kit takes a dark-only system as `defaultLight` with no `defaultDark`, so the page no longer pins `color-scheme`; `installThemes()` from `@agentic/ui/design-system` seeds zero's registry. The handoff's `--ag-*` properties (line, line-strong, text-muted, text-dim, link-hover, agent-1..4, radius-xl, control-h, input-h, control-h-touch, touch-min, pill-h, sidebar-w, topbar-h, space-8) are kit `custom` tokens; product states ride a `tone` axis (`data-tone`: muted | dim | live | working | needs-you | failed) and a `kind` axis (approval | input | interrupted | offline | machine | auth | runtime | task), presence flags ride modifiers (`hollow`, `outline`, `compact`, `selected`, `current`) — never `data-state`, whose vocabulary zero governs. The design system is derived from zero-daisyui's with the kit's `extendDesignSystem` (`@sigx/zero-kit/define`): the `control-room` tokens patched over daisy's (daisy's themes dropped; breakpoints daisy's sm / md / lg plus `xl: 80rem`, the handoff's 1280 regime, so `tokens.css` declares `--breakpoint-xl` and `css/breakpoints` `--above-xl` / `--below-xl`), one `RecipePatch` per re-tuned scope in `design-system/patches/<scope>.ts` (one recipe per scope, never two; the empty patches are registered stubs a later issue fills), the fragment, kit and code packs as `addRecipes`; the layout tier is regenerated from the derived tokens. The patches reach only daisy's declared `hooks` (the validator warns on a private name). daisy's `api` is carried, so the build also emits `dist/ds/components.js`; pages keep `@sigx/zero-daisyui/components` until a follow-up exports and re-points them. The web build runs `sigx zero:validate --package @agentic/ui --extra-manifest @agentic/ui --report --strict`: the design system by package name (its `./design-system` export), with the fragment merged through the package's `"sigx-zero"` field. The kit contrast-checks role and base pairs and, through `tokens.contrast`, the handoff's floors over the custom inks (text-dim on base-300 and text-muted on base-200 at 4.5, base-content on base-100 at 7, agent-1..4 on base-200 at 3, primary / info / warning / error on base-100 at 4.5) in every theme, so a regressed ink fails the build and `zero:validate --strict`. Fonts (Schibsted Grotesk, JetBrains Mono) load from Google Fonts through `useHead` links in `App.tsx`.
- Ops pages (`apps/web/src/pages/{Machines,Machine,Pair,Schedules,Plugins,Settings,History,Usage}.tsx`, #90): each is a `*View` component over props plus a thin route component that reads `apps/web/src/mock/ops.ts`, so the actor issues (#36, #42, #48, #44, #45) swap the data source without touching the layout. `pages/ops/OpsPage` carries `data-page` for `styles/pages.css`, a visually hidden `h1[data-page-title]` (the breadcrumb is the title; Pair shows a hero) and one head row for page actions; `pages/ops/LinkButton` is a real `<a href>` stamped with the button anatomy. The Machine page's `SessionsTable` is the agent Sessions tab's table too. Rows that carry `data-*` are raw `<tr data-scope="table" data-part="row">` because zero's `Table.Row` forwards only `class`.
- Component kit (`packages/ui/src/kit`): the handoff's app components, one visual per domain state. The `ag-*` scopes join the fragment with anatomies and recipes (`kitAnatomies` is the list) — `ag-env-line` (`machine / runtime / account`, never one part alone), `ag-needs-item` (`kind` approval | input | interrupted, `compact`), `ag-task-node` (depth rail, `selected`), `ag-connection`, `ag-version` (current | proposed | past), `ag-env-card` (`EnvironmentCard`: capacity meter, queued count, default-for tiles, auth fix line), `ag-markdown` (`MarkdownViewer`, #490: the prose recipe over `@sigx/richtext/dom`'s parts (`data-scope="richtext"`), which ship unstyled — headings, lists, task boxes, blockquotes, code with a shared lazy shiki highlighter, tables; `compact` for a card's well; the plan card's well and its `Open plan` dialog). `tokens.scopes` carries each scope's vocabulary claim (`kitScopes`). States (`kit/states`, #87) add `ag-banner` (`OfflineBanner`), plus `EventsLostRow`, the `ConnectionStrip` row helpers and the skeleton presets; with every declared axis value wired, the web build's `zero:validate` runs `--strict`. Compositions over zero carry no scope of their own; their look is the design system's patch for the zero scope (#591), and a tone reaches a zero part as its role (`roleOf`: live → primary, working → info, needs-you → warning, failed → error, muted / dim → neutral) with `data-tone` kept on the root: `StatusPill` / `Tag` are `Badge` (`soft`, or `outline` when hollow or a tag; `Badge.Dot running` while working; `PILLS` maps every `TaskStatus`, machine presence, auth status and tool phase to a tone, a solid or hollow dot and a label), `AgentTile` is `Avatar` (`shape` square for an agent, circle for a person; `data-hue` / `data-tile` set `--ag-hue` / `--ag-tile` in the avatar patch), `FailureCard` (the six named failure states of OPS-04 from one `FAILURES` table — name, icon, signal caption, tone, action; `color` the role, the `kind` axis the declared spelling, `client-offline` → `offline`) and `EmptyState` (variants; `compact` / `outline` as `data-mod-*`) are zero's `EmptyState`, `QuotaMeter`'s bar is `Progress`, `FilterChips` is a `ToggleGroup` filter bar (the chip look keyed on `data-filter-chips`), `Button` maps the five intents to daisy `color` / `variant` / `mods` (primary = primary solid, default = neutral solid, wait = warning solid, danger = error outline then solid at the confirm step, icon = neutral square with a required label), `Segmented` is a `ToggleGroup` with `aria-pressed` and a per-segment tone through the group's accent tokens, `DataTable` turns the artboard's column template into `<col>` widths on zero's `Table`, `ConfirmDialog` is zero's alert-dialog preset listing every dependent before a button that states the consequence, `MarkdownDialog` (#490) zero's `Dialog` at 880 px with a `MarkdownViewer` scrolling inside and a footer slot, `TimelineList` on `Timeline` (a marker's `color` is its entry's role), `Switch`, `ChipInput` (`MultiSelect`: zero's `Combobox multiple` over `FieldOption`s, #513), `SectionHeading`, `Label`, `Icon` (the artboards' geometry). The root exports `componentExportName(scope)` for every `ag-*` scope (`AgEnvLine`, `AgNeedsItem`, …).

- Inbox page (#40, `apps/web/src/pages/inbox`): "Needs you" is `NeedsYou` over a `NeedsSource` — rows from the Inbox `list` (unread `approval` / `input` with a session ref; the mock adds interrupted), each row's card over `Session.request(id)`, answers through `Session.respond` with `once` | `session` from the `ai-approval` card or `{ type: 'input' }` from the `ai-question` card (#194: one block per question of the request's `schema`, answers keyed by property — Claude Code's `AskUserQuestion` reads nothing else; `Session.respond` fits a plain string to the form). The chat renders the same card on the tool call that asked (`ToolCall`) and loose input requests at the thread's end. `liveNeedsSource(defs, workspaceId)` runs both reads `{ live: true }` (a decision anywhere drops the row and collapses the card everywhere), `memoryNeedsSource` is the same contract for the mock and the tests, `useNeedsSource` picks by `data-mode.ts` (`live`: `useActorDefs` — `Inbox` is in `ActorDefs` — and `useViewer`; else the mock). The chat's approval card (`Thread`'s `onRespond`) is wired the same way by the chat page.
- Live data via `useActorState(Def, key, 'get', {live: true})`; transcripts via `Session.tail` into `createReducer` / `reduceAgentEvent` (in-place mutation, one signal per part); markdown via `@sigx/richtext/dom`'s `RichTextView` with `markdownFormat`.
- Live data via `useActorState(Def, key, 'get', {live: true})`; transcripts via `Session.tail` into `createReducer` / `reduceAgentEvent` (in-place mutation, one signal per part); markdown via `@sigx/richtext/dom`'s `RichTextView` with `markdownFormat`. Wired for chats and sessions (#34, `apps/web/src/actors`, `pages/chat/{live,feeds,directory,LiveChat,LiveChats}`, `pages/session/{live,LiveSession}`): the platform's definitions are factory-built, not `*.actor.ts` modules, so the browser gets hand-built `__actorRef` stubs (`clientDefs()`) and SSR the registry's own objects (`platformDefs()`) through the `useActorDefs` injectable each entry provides; the page learns its workspace from the `whoami` serverFn (`useViewer`). Calls and the `$live` stream ride the Worker's HTTP actor mount (`actorsPlugin({ transport })`; the object-terminated socket is a follow-up). A send is `runActivation` (`pages/chat/live.ts`): `Chat.post`, then `Task.create` and `Routing.run` per activated agent — `run` dispatched **one-way** (#492, `.with({ oneWay: true })`, the `x-sigx-one-way` header): the composer clears once the tasks exist and the placement runs on the router's own time; how it went is on the live task index and in the chat's entries (`task-failed`, `session-started`), never in the send's own error. `Chat.get` and `Chat.history` are live reads — the newest page of entries (`HISTORY_LIMIT` = 200, the actor's maximum) on open, and the page before the oldest held through `Chat.history(cursor)` each time the reader reaches the top of the thread (`Thread.hasEarlier` / `onEarlier`, the chip or a scroll to within the threshold of the top), until `next` is `null` at the caller's `historyFrom`; the pages merge by `seq` into one list (`keepEntries`, #398). Each `sessions` row becomes a `connectSession` feed whose transport is the Session actor (`get` → `hello`, `tail` → event frames, commands → the actor's idempotent methods), anchored at the record's `transcriptAt` (`feedAnchor`) so it carries the turn running now — a reload mid-turn resumes it from the log — and never the session's earlier turns, whose final messages are entries (§6); `composeTranscript` folds the chat's entries and every feed's in-flight rows into the one transcript the Thread renders. The members panel reads ACTIVE from a member's in-flight task in this chat's tree (`workingAgents`: running, or waiting on a delegated child), WAITING from an open request in the entries, and idle otherwise — never from the session it holds, which every member holds for the life of the chat; a question from an earlier turn that is still open (`detachedQuestions`) is answered through the session the Inbox row names, else the member's bound one. A post is `Chat.post(text, mentions)` → one `Task.create` per activated agent (origin = the message, context = the entries visible to that agent) → `Routing.run`, from the browser (`runActivation`). Mock mode (`data-mode.ts`) keeps the design track's workspace for the dev server and Playwright.
- The chat's one transcript (`composeTranscript`, `pages/chat/live.ts`) is `running` only while a session is mid-turn **and** has a row of that turn to show (`inFlightMessages`). The Thread puts its streaming mark on the last assistant row, so a turn with nothing of its own yet, such as an input-less one a runtime opened by itself (#606, signalxjs/ai#193), must not make the chat mid-turn: the mark would land on a reply that finished long ago. `awaiting` still follows any session with an open question. A feed whose tail drops is followed again by `connectSession` itself, from its cursor.
- Agents on the platform (#35, `pages/agent/{live,head,LiveAgents,LiveAgent,NewAgentDialog}`): `/agents` reads the Workspace index live and each agent's identity through the chat directory (`Agent.get`); "New agent" (topbar → `newAgentRequest`) is `Workspace.createAgent` then `Agent.update(newAgentPatch, 'Created in the web UI')` — v1 on `anthropic-api` with `offlinePolicy: fail` — and lands on `/agents/:id?tab=config`. `/agents/:id` folds `Agent.get` (live) and `listVersions` (re-read per `configVersion`) into the same `AgentProfile` the mock tabs render (`profileOf`); the Config tab takes a `ConfigStore` so a save is `Agent.update(configPatch(config), reason)` and a rollback `Agent.rollback(v)`, the rail showing the version the actor recorded; "Start chat" is `createChatWith(defs, ws, [id], null)`. Demo 1 is proven three ways: `__tests__/pages/demo1-live.test.tsx` (the pages on the live harness, mock model), `__tests__/workers/demo1.test.ts` (dev login + the actors in workerd), and `e2e/demo1.spec.ts` through `smoke:demo1` against a deployed Worker (`docs/runbook.md`). The preview / local-only `/auth/dev-login` (`src/auth/dev-login.ts`) exists only while `AGENTIC_DEV_LOGIN` is set and mints a `dev_<user>` session, its own id namespace beside `gh_<id>`: `POST` JSON `{ token, user }` for the smokes, `GET` a plain HTML form (the secret prefilled from `?token=` on localhost only) that POSTs itself and lands on `/` (#143). The Worker records which doors are open per request (`src/auth/sign-in.ts` ← `entry.cloudflare.ts`); the `signInOptions` server function tells the shell, whose sidebar foot shows "Dev login" / "Sign in with GitHub" to a signed-out visitor in live mode. `pnpm dev` (`scripts/dev.mjs`) generates `apps/web/.dev.vars`, builds when the sources are newer than `apps/web/dist`, starts `wrangler dev` and prints the link (`docs/runbook.md` §4).
- Provider limits (#270, part of #261): `QuotaMeter` / `QuotaPanel` (`ag-quota`, `ag-quota-panel`) draw a `QuotaSnapshot` the way Claude Code's `/usage` does. Status is the tone; the reset time is in the viewer's zone; a panel older than 30 min is `stale`; `not-reported` and "nothing yet" are explicit (OPS-07). The panels appear on every Machines environment card (`EnvironmentCard.quota` ← `MachineView.quota`), in the `/usage` "Limits" card (one panel per account, `pages/usage/Limits.tsx`: `LiveLimits` reads the machine index, then `Machine.get` per paired machine), and in Home's rail (the tightest window per account, `compact`). **Where work is assigned (#315):** `QuotaBadge` is a one-line `QuotaMeter compact`: a short label (`Week · Fable`), a 56 px bar and the percent, with the full label and reset time as a tooltip; given the member's `model` it picks the tightest of `memberWindows` (the shared windows plus that model's own — another model's week is never its limit, #452; without a model, the shared windows only). It appears in three places:
