  - **Chat members:** each member of a chat shows the account of the environment it runs on there (its working folder's environment, else its default: `pages/chat/quota.ts`) for the model it runs (`member.options.model`, else its config's, #452): `QuotaRings` (`ag-quota-rings`) draws a ring per session / week window of `memberWindows` (`12% SESSION`, `77% WEEK`, `100% FABLE`), an amber line names the limit that ran out (`Fable limit · resets Thu 12:00`), and **Details** opens the account's whole `QuotaPanel` inline (`zoneInHeader`: the zone once in the header, each window `Resets Thu 25 Sep 12:00`). A note (no plan limits, nothing reported) stays the badge.
  - **Member model and mode (#453):** the card's model and mode rows are buttons like the folder's; each opens a listbox under the group ("Model · applies to the next turn") — the models the member's environment reports (`WorkdirEnvironment.models`), else the runtime plugin's `defaultModel` enum; the plugin's `defaultPermissionMode` enum, `bypassPermissions` only where the environment sets `allowBypassPermissions` (`pages/chat/member-options.ts`). A pick is `Chat.setOptions`; `×` clears it back to the config.
  - **Folder picker:** the working-folder picker's environment strip shows it per environment (`WorkdirEnvironment.quota`, from `EnvironmentEntry.quota` ← `MachineView.quota`).
  - **New chat:** the dialog picks members from cards showing where each agent runs and that account's limits, with the coordinator as a radio on the picked cards (`pages/chat/MemberPicker.tsx`, shared with the project form). **The machine (#414, `NewChatDialog.tsx`):** a "Machine" radio over the paired machines (`WorkdirEnvironments.machines()`, online first, `name · os`, offline tagged) preselected by `openingMachine` — the machine reporting a prefill's environment, else `Workspace.get().lastMachineId`, else the first online; under each option every picked daemon member reads "<account> signed in" or a `needs-you` "not signed in here" (`memberEnvironmentOn`: the pin as the machine reports it, else the account's login there through core's `environmentsForAccount`; a warning line under the chosen machine, never a block — the router says it at run); the cards' quota badges read the account's environment on the chosen machine (`quotaEnvironmentOf`); `NewChatCreate.machineId` (`null` with no machine to pick) → `Workspace.createChat({ projectId, machineId })` through `createChatWith`; `createChatFrom` sets a prefilled folder on the members that run in its environment ON that machine (`memberEnvironmentFor`). "Start chat" on an agent's page and "Start task" pass `lastMachineId` (Start task: the folder's machine when one was picked).
  - **Projects (#333, `pages/projects/{model,live,locate,head,ProjectsView,ProjectForm,LiveProjects}`, `pages/Projects.tsx`):** `/projects` lists `Workspace.projects()` live (name, members, one badge per place with a folder — `projectPlaces`, #702: the machine's name for its folder, the environment's label for an override, and for a pre-#702 folder the environment on the machine whose roots hold it — the enabled features); `/projects/new` and `/projects/:id` are one `ProjectForm` — name, description, the roster on the member cards, connectors as chips over the Registry's connector plugins, one folder row per paired machine (#702, `WorkdirEnvironments.projectMachines()` — environment ids are only unique per machine) with a `WorkdirInput` over that machine's environments, and on demand an override row per environment on it (placeholder: the machine's folder when its roots hold it, else a note that it is outside them); a pre-#702 folder or a `/projects/new?env=` prefill is keyed by the bare environment id until `resolveFolders` places it on the machine whose environment of that id holds it (the picker's `select` now carries the folder's git badge, `WorkdirSelection`), and every enabled `project-feature` manifest as a switch whose settings render from `projectSettings` on `SchemaForm`. **Find** on an empty row runs `Machine.fsRequest(env, { kind: 'locate', origin })` with the origin of another row's badge and reads `fsResult` live (`useLiveLocate`, the picker's deadline); the matches (path + branch) are offered in a dialog and a pick fills the row; a row whose checkout has another origin warns (`sameOrigin`) and never blocks. A plugin's `detect` (`projectFeatureCatalogue`, `plugins/catalogue.ts`; empty until #335) marks the row and preselects the feature when the project has none; a schema with an `origin` property is prefilled from the first badge. Save is `Workspace.upsertProject(patch)` — `projectPatchOf` sends every section whole with dropped folders / features as `null` — its 400 inline; delete is `removeProject`. New chat opens on a project picker preselected from `Workspace.get().lastProjectId`: picking one fills the roster and the coordinator from `members` and states its connectors and folders; `createChatWith(..., projectId)` is `Workspace.createChat({ projectId })`. The chat header shows the project chip (`ChatSummary.project` → `chatHead.project`, linking to `/projects/:id`) and the list column filters by project (`MockChatSummary.projectId`).

  A model-runtime member says "No plan limits · API key". Nothing here moves work (EXE-12).
- Machines on the platform (#144, `pages/machines/{live,head,environments,LiveMachines,LiveMachine,LivePair}`): `/machines` reads `Workspace.listMachines` live and, per paired entry, `Machine.get` live (`machineOf`: the daemon's name at `pair` over the index's, `online` false once revoked) — a socket drop flips the group without a reload; `Routing.get` supplies the EXE-12 queued counts (`waiting-offline` routes per environment); the chat directory supplies the "Default for" tiles (`defaultForByEnvironment`, from `identityOf`'s environment line — and, given the machine's environments, every account-bound agent under each environment whose login is its account, #414) and the platform row's agents, whose key pill reads NOT CHECKED (the deployment's key serves every workspace). `/machines/:id` reads `Machine.get` and `Machine.doctor` live (`doctorChecksOf`: isolation across the environments, then per environment its sign-in and the daemon's verdict with its findings — an unverified environment is a failed "not checked" row), `Task.get` per hosted session for the objective (`sessionsOf`: `opening` reads AWAITING, and each row's `load` from `MachineView.telemetry` — the CPU and Memory columns read `unknown` for a session the daemon could not attribute, `—` before any telemetry, the memory cell amber over `TELEMETRY_LIMITS.sessionRss`, #400); the machine's own load (`machineLoadOf`: `CPU 34 % · 12 GB of 32 GB in use` after the heartbeat in the hero caption and in the `/machines` group head, amber while a limit is crossed, dim when the sample is older than `TELEMETRY_STALE_MS`, `load not reported` with the daemon's reason as the tooltip when it is off) and, while `telemetryWarnings` names any, an alert under the hero (`warningText`: the agent and session, the number, the level — and that nothing is stopped); each environment card's `load` (`EnvironmentCard` `load` part: `CPU 12 % · 1.8 GB`, `idle`, or `load unknown` with the attribution as the tooltip); Revoke is `Machine.revoke` (REVOKED in the hero, the card stops offering it); "Run again" re-reads the stored verdicts because the platform cannot run the daemon's doctor; an unpaired id gets a not-paired card; the topbar crumb reads `machineHead`. `/pair` mints through `Workspace.registerMachinePending({ name })` on the client once the workspace is known (`machine-N` by default; a rename mints again so the index entry and the daemon's `--name` agree; "New code" mints again), shows the runbook's `install.ps1` line and `agentic-daemon pair <code> --url <origin> --name <name>`, and watches the pending record live — `paired` moves the page to `/machines/:id`. The agent Config tab's environment picker (`useEnvironmentOptions`: the index live, then `Machine.get` per paired machine) offers every environment as `machine / runtime / account` and a save persists `execution.defaultEnvironmentId`. `MachineView`, `MachineGroup`, `EnvironmentGrid`, `PlatformRow`, `SessionsTable` and `PairView` take their facts (queued counts, default-for tiles, agent lookup, commands, revoked state) as props, so the mock pages pass the sample workspace's and the live pages the actors'. Tests: `__tests__/pages/machines-live.test.tsx` on the live harness, `__tests__/workers/machines-pages.test.ts` in workerd (the pair route mounted as the actor mount's fallback so its hop to the Machine object runs under the Worker's host scope, #137).
- Home, Tasks, History and Usage on the platform (#146, `pages/task/{live,LiveTasks}`, `pages/history/{live,LiveHistory}`, `pages/usage/{live,LiveUsage}`, `LiveHome` in `Home.tsx`): `/tasks` and Home's active tasks are ONE live read of the workspace's `TaskIndex` (`list()`; every Task actor writes its row over a hop on create and every status / wait change — §4), each row's assignee through the chat directory, "Stop all" = `Task.cancel('user')` per chain root (`chainRoots`: an active task whose parent is not itself active; the cascade covers the subtree). `/history` is `Audit.list(query)`: the kind chips, the agent select and the time window are the actor's `kinds` / `agentId` / `since`, "Load older" passes the page's `seq` cursor and the pages merge by event key; a read that takes an options object cannot be a `useActorState` key (primitives only), so the page subscribes to `Audit.stats()` live and re-keys its `useData` first page on `recorded`. `/usage` is `Ledger.summary({ by: agent | task | day, timeZone })` over the current UTC month (`ledgerMonthOf`) through `useData`, re-read on mount and on Refresh; a cost is printed as its reported part plus `~` its estimated part (`costPartsOf` / `costText`), `n/a` in the dim ink when no row was priced (OPS-07), the quality pill per group; Home's spend panel reads the same total (`useMonthSpend`). Home's "Today" panel does not read the schedules yet. `ActorDefs` carries `TaskIndex`, `Audit`, `Ledger`; the platform stays out of the client bundle (types only).
- Zero components per screen: shell `Navbar` + `Drawer`; chat `Chat` rows + `Avatar` / `Status` / `Badge`; composer `Textarea` (`modelModifiers`) + `Button` + `FileUpload` + `Kbd`; tasks `Table` / `TreeView` / `Timeline` / `Steps`; approvals `Dialog` / `Alert`; inbox `Toast`; settings `Field` + `Input` / `Select` / `Switch` / `Combobox`; cost `Stats`.
- `@agentic/ui` fragment scopes, styled to `docs/design/HANDOFF.md` → "`ai-*` fragment": `ai-thread` (root/earlier/list/row/anchor, `data-state` on | off; the "Showing the last N entries · Load earlier" chip), `ai-message` (root/avatar/meta/name/environment/time/body/image/file/file-name/file-size/tools/footer — an `image` part is a lazy ≤ 320 px thumbnail linking to the full size, a `file` part a download chip, placement start | end — a row on the kit's `AgentTile` and `EnvironmentLine`, the STREAMING pill mid-turn; who an author is comes from the page through `Thread.describe`), `ai-composer` (root/addressing/recipient/hint/attachments/attachment/thumbnail/attachment-name/attachment-size/spinner/input/actions/keys, over zero `Textarea` (autosizing between `minRows` / `maxRows`, Enter-to-send on its own `onKeydown`, the element through its `TextareaHandle`) inside a `Combobox trigger="@"` for mentions (zero runs the popup's keys first, so Enter with the popup open picks and never sends; the list is `filterMentions`' ranked, capped one with `filter={false}`), the kit `Button`, zero `Kbd` in the key hint and the kit `ErrorNote` on a failed chip; the page resolves `recipients` per CHT-06; files arrive by the Attach button's hidden `<input type=file multiple>`, by paste (`clipboardData.files`) and by drag-and-drop — the root takes the governed `highlighted` flag while a drag hovers, as zero's `FileUpload` does — and all three emit one `files: File[]` event: the composer is presentational and the host owns the upload (CHT-10, #207), feeding back `attachments` whose `status` `uploading | ready | error` is the chip's `data-state` `loading | complete | error`; Send waits while any chip uploads and may go with an empty draft; `prepareImage` downscales to 1568 px and re-encodes as WebP / JPEG before upload when that is smaller), `ai-tool-call` (root/header/icon/name/signature/meta/status/input/output/more/log/error/agent; `input`, `output` and the agent's work fold on zero `Collapsible`), `ai-reasoning` (root/summary/body; the root holds a zero `Collapsible` whose `model` follows the part until the reader toggles it, `thread/disclosure.ts`), `ai-approval` (root/header/title/rule/request/description/context/actions/record, `compact` modifier; one card everywhere — chat, inbox, task, session, mobile). The tool lifecycle is spelled in zero's governed lifecycle states — `loading | running | paused | complete | error | denied | cancelled` (a sub-agent's `paused` and `cancelled` too) — with the handoff's word on a `StatusPill` in `status` and the refined phase in `meta`; the border takes a colour only for `running` and `error`, and the look-alike pairs are declared with `sameAs`. The pack stays on the recommended grammar (the `--ag-*` inks are read with fallbacks; state colour rides `data-state` and nested kit scopes, never a `tone` axis of its own), so it remains promotable as is. Declared with `"sigx-zero": { "fragment": "./dist/fragment.js", "requires": ">=0.5.0" }` and a recipe pack; `version` is `FRAGMENT_VERSION` from `@sigx/zero/contract`. The package build runs `sigx zero:fragment --strict`, the authoring gate (fragment schema, the merge into zero's manifest, recipes confined to declared parts, the lynx probe, `"files"`, `componentExportName(scope)` on the root) that also writes `dist/fragment.json`; `packages/ui/__tests__/fragment.test.ts` keeps only the product assertions. The lynx probe compiles the pack against a vocabulary with no spacing, typography or `--ag-*` tokens and refuses any `var()` it cannot resolve, so the exported pack goes through `withFallbacks` (`fragment/fallbacks.ts`): each bare `var(--x)` of the kit vocabulary (at zero-daisyui's defaults, plus agentic's extra steps) or an `--ag-*` token (at the `control-room` values) becomes `var(--x, <fallback>)`. The design system compiles the recipe files untouched, since it defines all of them. agentic owns its design system, so it will not use `sigx zero:extend`. The thread windows by PART (a message of 2 000 parts renders its last 150), keys rows by message id so a sliding window patches rather than remounts, and freezes the window while the reader is scrolled up.
- Responsive: pages lay out with zero's layout tier (`Row`, `Col`, `Stack.Item grow` from `@sigx/zero`), whose CSS the design system build compiles from its own spacing ramp and breakpoints (#584). The shell (`packages/ui/src/shell`, `AppShell`, #589) is the handoff's on zero's app-shell composition: the navigation renders once, in one `Drawer.Root modal={{ below: 'md' }}` — docked inline from md up as the sticky 232 px sidebar (`--ag-sidebar-w`), a 312 px modal sheet below behind a 44 px `Drawer.Trigger` (50 px items, connection strip at the foot, daisy's slide, still under reduced motion), a sheet left open closing itself when the viewport widens. The server renders the drawer docked (`open` in markup) and the design system's compiled structure CSS hides whichever half the viewport disagrees with, so neither width flashes; trigger and close hide while docked. The panel holds brand, one `NavList.Root label="Main"` (the only navigation landmark: `NavList.Group` per nav group — Primary named by its label, Workspace by its `NavList.Heading` — `NavList.Link current` for `aria-current="page"`, `NavList.Icon`, the Home count a warning `Badge` with an accessible label in `NavList.Meta`), then the `connection` and `user` slots, and a visually hidden `Drawer.Title`. Beside it a 60 px topbar (`--ag-topbar-h`; `breadcrumb` and `actions` slots on zero's `Navbar`, the banner) and `<main>` (padding 28, `flush` for Chat); below 768 px the topbar is a 60 px app bar on base-200 — menu or `back`, `title` with the `subtitle` slot, one right slot (`phoneAction`, else the last action). The drawer's and the list's look are the `drawer` and `nav-list` patches (`src/design-system/patches/`); `shell.css` keeps the page grid, the sticky docked column, the bar, brand, connection and user, and queries `(--below-md)` / `(--above-md)`. Router-agnostic: the app renders its link through the `link` slot, spreading the `NavList.Link` part props it is handed (`props`, plus `icon` and `meta`), and the `back` slot. Per-screen grids live in `apps/web/src/styles/pages/<area>.css` (shared, home, inbox, chat, projects, tasks, sessions, agents, machines, ops, plugins, history, usage), which `styles/pages.css` imports in cascade order; their media queries use the design system's `@custom-media` (`@import '@agentic/ui/css/breakpoints'`, `@media (--below-md)`, `(--below-xl)`), compiled by Lightning CSS (`css.transformer: 'lightningcss'` with `drafts.customMedia` in `apps/web/vite.config.ts`). Each area file carries its share of the two responsive blocks of the handoff's single 768 px breakpoint: 768–1279 the fixed rails drop under the main column (Home, Task, Session, Machine, Memory), Chat keeps list + thread with the context panel in an end drawer behind the topbar's tasks button, three-column card grids become two; below 768 every grid is one column, `DataTable` stacks its rows into captioned cards from CSS alone (`--ag-col-N` on its wrapper, no second markup), the composer docks with 48 px controls, buttons are 48 px and split a row evenly, the environment card folds to a 52 px row, the message meta drops its environment line. The topbar is the route's: `apps/web/src/crumbs.ts` builds the trail (the section root plus the page's entity name from its `defineTopbar` contribution) that is the one-line breadcrumb at ≥ 768 px and the app bar's title + back link below; pages register their actions, sub-line and phone action in `components/topbar.ts`, never in an in-content head row. Playwright runs at 400 / 1024 / 1280 (`apps/web/e2e`, the CI `e2e` job). `themeInitScript` reaches `<head>` through `useHead`; `installThemes()` runs once per entry; a per-request `ThemeProvider` serves `useTheme()` on the server. The app's `build` runs `sigx zero:validate` against the consumed design system first, so CI validates it. The thread windows its own rows.
- Zero workarounds still in the tree, one per line. Each line names the issue that removes it and deletes the line. zero 0.5.0 replaced the rest, closed out in #595: the design-system audit is 0 errors / 0 warnings and `__tests__/audit.test.ts` holds it there.
  - page form dialogs on `ConfirmDialog danger={false}`: to be replaced by the kit `FormDialog` (#691)
  - `<p data-chat-error role="alert">` errors and a few raw form controls in the plugins, chat and session pages: to be replaced by `ErrorNote` and zero form parts (#691)
  - `/usage` stat cards as one `Stats.Root color` per card: to become one `Stats.Root` with a `color` per `Stats.Item` once zero ships that axis (signalxjs/zero#161)
  - the Connectors view's chip buttons, drawn by a base rule moved from `history.css` into `styles/plugins/connectors.css`: to be replaced by the kit `FilterChips` (#687)
  - the published `@agentic/ui/fragment` pack loses its `below-<bp>` keys (`_zero-gaps/pack-breakpoints.ts`), because `sigx zero:fragment`'s probe declares no breakpoints and refuses them. The `agentic` design system compiles the recipes whole (signalxjs/zero#225)
- Design system after #595: every responsive rule is a recipe `below-md` / `below-xl` key or `composes` (no hand-written width in `packages/ui/src`); `DataTable` is `Table.Root columns` + `stack="md"` (callers name each cell's `column`); the pack declares out the colour, size and part-level tone/kind axes it never wires (`fragment/scopes.ts`, the fragment's `scopes` export, merged under the kit's claims in `tokens.scopes`); look-alike states are `sameAs`; spacing is on the ramp (`calc()` of steps where the handoff draws between them); `ai-form` and `ai-shell` are declared anatomies with small recipes, and the components stamp `FORM_SCOPE` / `SHELL_SCOPE`.
- Forms (`packages/ui/src/forms`): every configuration surface edits a draft bound through zero's `model=` contract (`Field` + labelled control, `Field.Error` wired by `aria-describedby`) and posts the same field names pre-hydration; one model module per form (`agent-model.ts`, `schema-model.ts`) owns config ⇄ draft ⇄ `FormData` and validation, so the live and posted paths cannot drift. A valid submit writes back through the model and emits; persistence (the Agent actor's versioned config, Workspace settings) is wired by the pages. The approval editor owns one rule per tool category (`id: category:<name>`, first in the policy); every other rule is carried through untouched. Multi-selects are `MultiSelect` / `MultiSelectField` (`forms/fields.tsx`, #513): zero's `Combobox multiple` over `FieldOption`s, the chosen values its tags (label, the `tag` slot — the agent form's per-tool mode `Select` — then remove), posting every value and every custom one through zero's hidden `<select multiple>`; the `_zero-gaps/multi-select.tsx` stopgap is gone. The Agent config page (`apps/web/src/pages/agent/ConfigTab.tsx`, #89) renders `AgentForm` in its `sections` layout with the segmented approval control and puts the save card (only while `dirty()`) and the versions rail (`VersionItem`: proposed / current / past) in the form's `rail` slot; the memory tab (`MemoryTab.tsx`) is the handoff's row grid over `MemoryEntry` with filter chips per kind; the sessions tab shares the Machine page's sessions table columns (`SESSIONS_COLS`). Agent pages read `apps/web/src/mock/agents.ts` until #35 / #41 wire the Agent and Memory actors.
- Schedules, Plugins and Settings on the platform (#145, `apps/web/src/pages/ops/{live,environments,head,LiveSchedules,NewScheduleDialog,LivePlugins,LiveSettings}`; `Schedule` and `Registry` join `ActorDefs`, `clientDefs` and `platformDefs`): the routes switch on `dataMode()` like the chat and agent pages, the mock `*View` untouched. `/schedules` reads the Workspace index live and each entry's `Schedule.get` as its own live read (a firing or a switch re-renders that row), on the workspace's `settings.timeZone` (AST-07: `nextRunLabel`, `whenLabel`, the DST footer); the switch is `Schedule.enable` / `disable`; "New schedule" (topbar → `newScheduleRequest`) is `Workspace.createSchedule` then `Schedule.create(newScheduleSpec(input, tz))` — a reminder at a wall time typed on the workspace clock (`wallToInstant` resolves it through `Intl` like `recur.ts`: a spring-gap time lands after the gap, a repeated fall time takes its first occurrence), or a cron recurrence, plain or as an agent task with an environment (`useEnvironmentDirectory`: the paired machines' `Machine.get().environments`, also the Settings default-environment select; an offline machine shows the policy line). `/plugins` reads the Registry's `list`, `connectors` and `secrets` (names only) live, `dependents(id)` per plugin for "Used by"; switching off asks `dependents` first and the dialog lists them by name (`dependentNames`: agents with how, schedules through their agent), the confirm is `Registry.disable`, whose answer the card states ("Still referenced by …; new use is refused, running work finishes" — AC-13 through the page). `/settings` edits a draft of `Workspace.get().settings` (time zone from `Intl.supportedValuesOf`, default environment, the workspace-wide inbox / push flags, retention in days) and Save is `Workspace.updateSettings(settingsPatch(draft))`; keys are the Registry's secret names; "Export workspace" is `Workspace.exportAll` with the record's outcome shown (`opStatus`), "Delete workspace" is `Workspace.deleteAll` behind a dialog that needs the workspace's name typed back. Tests: `__tests__/pages/ops-live.test.tsx` on the live harness (which now takes extra `actors` and host `defaults`; a reminder created on the page fires from the entry's own reminder into the Inbox, the plugin dialog and card, the settings round trip, an export into a fake sink with its manifest, delete-all refusing the wrong name), `ops-live-model.test.ts`, `__tests__/workers/ops-pages.test.ts` (the browser stubs against the Schedule and Registry objects; an alarm run early re-arms).
- **Plugins redesign — target (#625; boards in `docs/design/plugins/`).** The card catalogue gives way to four views, each with its URL as the state so a view can be linked and reloaded:
  - `/plugins?kind=&status=&q=`: all plugins (#637). `kind` is the category menu's item (a kind from `KIND_ORDER`, runtimes split by `RUNTIME_KIND_ORDER`, or Needs attention; none is All plugins); `status` is a status chip (All, On, Off, Needs setup); `q` is the search over name, description, id, tool names and permission scopes. The three combine. Rows link to `/plugins/:id`.
  - `/plugins?kind=connector`: the Connectors view (#638), only the connectors the workspace has, with `Add connector`.
  - `/plugins/connectors/add?category=&q=&selected=`: Add a connector (#639), a page and never a dialog. `category` and `q` filter the static listings; `selected` is the listing in the preview panel; `show=not-connected` hides what is connected. It is registered before `/plugins/:id`, so `connectors` is never read as a plugin id. Built (#639, `pages/plugins/add/`): Connect on a conduit listing turns its plugin on (`Registry.enable`) and goes to the sign-in start route with `?next=agents`, which rides on the conduit `returnTo`; the callback (`afterConnect` in `connectors/routes.ts`) then lands on `?selected=<id>&next=agents` instead of the plugin page. An MCP listing opens the MCP form prefilled from `mcpPreset` and runs `addConnector()`. `next=agents` (plus `plugin=` when the id is not the listing's) is the step that appends `{ id }` to each chosen agent's `AgentConfig.connectors` through `Agent.update(configPatch(config), "Added <name> from Plugins")` — none chosen by default, and no grant is touched (PLG-04) — then routes to `/plugins/:id`.
  - `/plugins/:id`: the plugin page (#640): account, per-tool allow / ask / deny (the workspace tool policy, §9), granted and declared scopes with Revoke / Grant, Used by, and Remove.
  - Responsive (#641, `styles/plugins/*.css`): below 768 px the category menu is a `Select` above the view (`PluginsLayout`), a row stacks tile, name over readiness, then the switch (kind, dependents and chevron drop), the add page is one column with the preview as a full-screen sheet and Connect docked at its foot, and every control is a 44 px target; from 768 to 1279 px the add page's preview drops under the tiles and the plugin page's rail under its main column. The old card catalogue, its connector list and the kit's plugin card are gone; `ReadinessBadge` owns the `ag-readiness` scope that replaced `ag-plugin-card`.
- Failure distinction (OPS-04) from four signals: client socket status, `Machine.online`, environment `authStatus`, adapter `error` events, `Task.status` — each rendered by its own `FailureCard` kind (`kit/states`), never a generic error. Recovery (OPS-05): interrupted turns are marked, never auto-replayed; "Resume" issues a new prompt over the intact transcript. Wired by #46 (`apps/web/src/components/status`): `failureOf(signals)` is the one pure mapping to exactly one state — the socket (`clientConnection`, fed by `watchTransport` around the actor transport and the browser's online events), then `Machine.online` / a `disconnected` session, then `authStatus` missing / expired or an auth-shaped doctor error, then the interrupted marker (the Session's `process_exited` turn end, the Task's `waiting {input, resume:…}`), then a non-recoverable adapter error, then `Task.status: failed` by its code (`taskFailureKind`: `environment-offline` → machine, `auth*` → auth, the adapter codes and `turn-error` / `prompt-*` → runtime, the rest — `session-open` / `no-api-key` included — → task). `FailureNotice` draws it with the one action; the shell strip (`LiveConnection`) and the offline banner read the socket, the chat (`chatFailure`: feeds first, then the `task-failed` / `interrupted:` status rows the router and the Session publish, #128), session (`sessionSignals`) and task (`LiveTask`) pages each map what they have. Resume is `Routing.resume(taskId)` → `Session.resume()` (a new `{turnId}:resume` prompt with the cut turn's input) and a `resumed` transition on the Task; the route parks as `interrupted` meanwhile; interrupted work reads uncertain on the card, the node and the transition until then.
  - **Causes and resume states (#368, `components/status/{interruption,reads}.ts`):** every interruption names why — "the daemon on alien01 restarted" (`restart`), "alien01 was updated" (`update`), "the claude-code harness on alien01 was updated" (`harness-update`), "the session on alien01 ended" (any other close code), and "the platform restarted mid-turn" when no row records one (an eviction). `interruptionOf` folds two live reads, `useInterruptionReads`: the Audit's newest `session.interrupted` / `session.resumed` / `task.machine-lost` rows (the host, the machine as `by: machine:{id}`, whether it went on and who asked — the router is "automatically"), read through `useData` keyed by the log's live `stats`, and `Routing.get()`'s route (`rehosting` → Resume disabled "Resuming…"; a frozen `onInterrupt: 'auto'` not yet spent on this cut → disabled "Resuming automatically"). The Session page reads the cause from its own log instead (`cutOfEvents`: the interrupted `error` event's `data.host`, a later `{turnId}:resume` turn = resumed). Where it shows: the card (`Interrupted: {cause}.`, signal `session.closed {host}`), the chat's `interrupted:{turnId}` row (`interruptionLine`: the cause, then `· resuming automatically` / `· resumed`), the Task and Session pages' "resumed" note once the card is gone, and the inbox row's context. The chat's Resume takes the task from the route when the member's feed has not named it.
  - **Machine waits (#366/#368):** a task waiting `machine-offline` is a wait, never a failure — "Waiting for alien01 (offline since 14:02); fails after 24 h" with Open machine under the Task page's tree and the chat's thread, and `wait: machine-offline · alien01 offline since 14:02 · fails after 24 h` on the node and in the Tasks table. `machine-lost` is the `machine` card naming the machine (`Task.error` message, else the id), with Retry where the page has one (the chat re-sends the last message) and the error is recoverable, else Open machine. An idle daemon session its online machine no longer hosts says "The machine lost this session; it re-opens with the next message."
  - **Capacity waits (#652; EXE-09):** a task of the chat's tree parked `wait: capacity` (the router's `waiting-capacity`) reads under the thread as "Waiting for a free slot on work (alien01): 1 of 1 turn running · 2nd in line" with **Change limit** (`chatWaitsOf` + `capacityWaitText`; the machine is the route's, read live only while one waits, and the running count is `activeIn` over it), and its member reads QUEUED (`queuedAgents`). The link is `/machines/:id?env=<envId>`: the machine page opens that environment's edit dialog, where **Turns at once** is the daemon's `concurrency`; with web management off it shows the `agentic-daemon env add … --concurrency <n> --replace` line instead (`concurrencyCommand`). The environment cards count running turns from the Machine's sessions (`liveCapacity`), not the daemon's last `concurrency.active`, and `queued` counts `waiting-capacity` routes beside `waiting-offline`.
  - **`onInterrupt` (#368):** `AgentForm` has "When a turn is interrupted" beside the offline select ("Ask me" / "Resume automatically, once", with the OPS-06 caveat); `agent-model.ts` writes only `auto`, and the agent page's `configPatch` always names `onInterrupt` (`ask` when absent) so switching back clears an `auto` the one-level merge would keep.
- Live plumbing (#150): `ActorDefs` also names `Memory` (a stub in `clientDefs()`, the registry's object in `platformDefs()`; key `memoryKeyOf(ws, scope)`). `__tests__/actors-keys.test.ts` pins `memoryKeyOf`, `taskIndexKeyOf`, `registryKeyOf` and `ledgerKeyOf` / `ledgerMonthOf` against the platform's helpers and holds the browser stubs and the SSR definitions to the same actor types, name by name. Times on the live pages: `src/time.ts` — `zoneFormat(zone)` (`time` / `dateTime` / `age`, built once per IANA zone, an unknown zone formats as UTC), `useWorkspaceZone(defs, viewer)` over a live `Workspace.get().settings.timeZone`, and `clockNow()` (the real clock in live mode, the mock workspace's frozen one otherwise). A formatter is passed into the pure adapters — there is no module-level zone, because two server renders share the module. `Age` takes `zone` and defaults `now` to `clockNow()`. The mock workspace keeps its own zone and clock.
- Chat on live data (#152, `apps/web/src/pages/chat`): everything the live chat pages print comes from an actor or from this device. **Tasks in this chat** — one live `TaskIndex.list()` folded by `chatTasks(rows, chatId)`: a root is a row whose `chatId` is this chat, delegated children (no `chatId`) hang off it by `parentId`; depth-first, a chain that still runs before a settled one, capped at `CHAT_TASKS_CAP`. **Stop task chain** — `Task.cancel('user')` on `stopTargets` (the highest task of each chain that has not settled; the cancel takes the subtree, COL-12); `notStoppedLine` names what a report could not stop on the page's error line; the button and the dialog list only what has not settled. **Waiting** — `openRequests(entries)` pairs `request` / `request-resolved` status entries by `ref`; an agent with one open reads WAITING in the members panel and the topbar summary, and its chat's list row carries the amber pill. **Unread** — device-local by decision: `read-marks.ts` keeps the chat's `seq` at the moment this device last had it open, per workspace, in `localStorage` (guarded; SSR and a refusing store count nothing); `unreadOf` counts agent messages at or past it; a chat first seen on a device starts at its present end; the open chat never counts. Chat state is a replayed entry log, so a marker there would need a core entry kind and spend the window on non-content — read state that follows the user is #157. **The list is live** — after the batch read for the first paint, one renderless `ChatWatch` per chat holds live reads of `Chat.get` and the newest `LIST_TAIL` (30) entries (the `MachineWatch` pattern; the `$live` channel multiplexes them), so a reply elsewhere moves the row, its count and its pill without a reload; a row's last line is the newest message (`lastOf`), not the session bookkeeping after it. **Search and settings** — the topbar's two buttons raise `chatSearchRequest` / `chatSettingsRequest` (`head.ts`, the `newChatRequest` seam) in live mode and stay inert in mock mode: a panel over `Chat.search(q, 20)` (hits are listed with who and when; the Thread's rows carry no DOM id to jump to), and a dialog whose `settingsChange` names only what changed — members leave first (`removeAgent`; a leaving coordinator takes the role with it), then `setCoordinator`, then `rename`. The chat list's search box filters rows by title and last line in both modes. The user reads as "You" (it was the mock workspace's person) and every time on the page is `zoneFormat(useWorkspaceZone()).time` (#150).
- Session page without literals (#154): `liveSessionView` takes the open time from `spec.retrieval.at` — the instant `Session.open` stamped, present when the platform has its learning ports; absent, the header says only where the session was opened from — and the working dir from the environment's first `cwdRoots` entry in the `MachineView` the page already reads (what the router hands the daemon); a platform session has none and the row is left out. The header's time is printed in the workspace zone (`SessionView`'s `time` prop ← `zoneFormat(useWorkspaceZone())`). The current tool call shows a duration only when the view carries one (`current.meta`, the design workspace's): agent events have no timestamps, so the platform reports none. Session grants are listed and never revocable (`SessionInfo.grants`), so `SessionView` draws Revoke only for an `onRevoke` nobody passes today, under the note "Grants end with the session."
- Shell and inbox on live data (#151): the sidebar's Home badge is the "Needs you" count — `App` reads `useNeedsSource()().useRows()` and passes the length to `NAV_GROUPS(badge)`, so the badge and Home's "N open" are one number from one source (not `Inbox.unread()`, which counts every kind and misses interrupted work); the mock shell keeps the design workspace's three. Interrupted work (OPS-05) is a row: `liveNeedsSource` adds the router's routes parked `interrupted` (`interruptedRows` over a live `Routing.get()` — the agent by the route's frozen config, the chat the task came from, else the task), and the row's Resume is `NeedsSource.resume(row)` → `Routing.resume(taskId)`; the row leaves with the live read once the route runs again (`memoryNeedsSource` drops it in memory). Ages print through `zoneFormat(zone).age(at, clockNow())` with the zone from `useWorkspaceZone`. A signed-in user gets a plain `<form method="post" action="/auth/logout">` in the shell's user slot, live mode only.
- Agent pages on live data (#153, `apps/web/src/pages/agent`): `activity.ts` holds the reads, `live.ts` the pure folds (`profileOf(view, versions, index, activity)`). Presence and the Sessions tab come from the workspace's `TaskIndex` — one live `list()` shared by the roster and the agent page and filtered by assignee in the browser (a live read's arguments are part of its key and must be JSON primitives, so the query object stays out): `presenceOf` (waiting on an approval or an input beats active; nothing in flight is idle), `sessionRowsOf` (the rows that carry a `sessionId`, one per session — every session is opened by the router for a task). `activeOnOlder` (AGT-07) is `Task.get().configVersion` of the tasks in flight against the current version. The Memory tab lists the agent's private scope (`Memory.exportPage`, re-read whenever `Memory.stats().rev` moves, so a write from anywhere reaches an open tab) and acts through a `MemoryTabStore` prop (`correct` → `Memory.update` as stated by the user, `retire` → `Memory.retire`, `remove?` → `Memory.delete`, `setLearning` → `Agent.update({ memoryPolicy })`, a config version); Delete is drawn only when the store has `remove` (the AC-15 rule), and without a store the tab mutates a local copy (the mock page). Counters: `Ledger.corrections(agent, isoWeek)` and the same for `wrong`, summed over the month ledgers the week has touched (`weekMonths`; a correction is tallied in the month it happened in); the roster's cards read their own `Memory.stats().live` and corrections (`CardStats`). The oldest pending instruction proposal is the rail's NEEDS REVIEW item (`proposedVersion`); `ConfigStore.review` accepts or rejects it through `Agent.reviewProposal`, and on an accept the form restarts from the config the actor holds, so a later save cannot undo the patch. The pickers take a `catalog` (`catalog.ts`): tools are `PLATFORM_TOOLS` (a mirror of `@agentic/runtimes`' `PLATFORM_TOOL_NAMES`, pinned by a test — the browser does not import the runtimes) plus what the Registry's connectors discovered, connectors are `Registry.connectors()`, skills and shared scopes only what the agent already carries (there is no catalogue of either), and `environments` is left to the machines wiring (#144). Times print in the workspace's zone against the real clock (`format.ts`, `useWorkspaceZone`).
- Working folders in the web app (#193, part of #185; AGT-05, EXE-02, EXE-06):
  - **`pages/workdir/`:** `WorkdirPicker` drives `@agentic/ui`'s `WorkdirDialog` (#191).
    - Live: a listing is `Machine.fsRequest(env, { kind: 'list', path })`, then `fsResult(requestId)` read live until the daemon's answer lands (#189). The last listing stays on screen while the next loads, and the picker gives up after `CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS`.
    - A worktree is the same round trip with `worktree`, and the picker then opens the new folder.
    - Recent folders are `Workspace.recentWorkdirs()`. On mock data the folders come from `mock/fs.ts`.
    - `WorkdirInput` is the field-plus-picker the forms use. `workdirEnvironmentOf` builds the environments (`machine / environment`, roots, the machine's OS, and why one is unavailable: offline, no roots, sign-in) from the environment directory. The directory (`pages/ops/environments.ts`, #414) also lists the paired machines with what they report (`machines()`, `MachineEntry`), the accounts across them (`accounts()`, core's `accountDirectory`), `hosted(machineId, envId)` / `lookupOn` (ids are minted per daemon, so an id alone is ambiguous), `accountEnvironment(machineId, runtime, ref)` (the environment the router would take) and `lastMachineId()`; `WorkdirEnvironments` carries the same for the pages that take that shape.
  - **Entry points:**
    - **Chat:** the context panel shows a folder chip per daemon member, which calls `Chat.setWorkdir`. **The machine (#414):** the topbar shows a machine chip beside the project chip (`ChatSummary.machine`, tone by `online` from the directory; the id alone for a removed machine); "Chat settings" has a "Machine" select (`ChatSettingsChange.machineId`, `settingsChange` names it only when it changed; `null` = none) → `Chat.setMachine`, with a note that each member starts a fresh session there at its next message, work in flight finishes where it runs, the history stays; the context panel reads each member's quota from its account's environment on the chat's machine (`memberQuota(…, on)`, "Not signed in on <machine>" when it has none) and marks a member folder whose environment the machine does not report as stale with a Clear (`hosted`); `runActivation` copies `summary.machineId` into every task and drops such a folder (`activationWorkdir`), as the router would. `activationContract` copies the member's folder into the task as `environmentId` + `workdir`, and the folder note renders by name. **Effective folder (#333):** the chip shows `member.workdir ?? projectFolderFor(project, member's environment)` (`effectiveWorkdir`), marked "from project" when inherited; Clear appears only for an override, and clearing goes back to the project's folder. `activationContract` copies the chat's `projectId` into the task, so the router resolves the project's folder wherever the member has none.
    - **`/chats/new?env=&path=&origin=` (#336, `pages/chat/{NewChatEntry,new-chat-prefill}`):** where `agentic-daemon open` sends the browser (§5b). The route (declared before `/chats/:id`, crumb Chats) renders the chats page and opens the New chat dialog on a prefill — `newChatPrefillOf(route.query)`: `env` and `path` required, `origin` optional, a malformed query is the plain dialog. `NewChatDialog.prefill`: with an `origin` that a project's git feature names (`projectForOrigin` over `features['agentic.feature.git'].origin`, core's `sameOrigin`) that project is preselected over the last used one and, when it has no folder on that environment, a checked line offers to save this one; with an origin no project names the dialog starts on "No project" and offers **Just this chat** (the default) or **Create project from this folder**; without an origin the last used project stays. `create` then carries `workdir: { environmentId, path, saveToProject }` and `createChatFrom` (`LiveChats`) runs `Workspace.upsertProject({ id, folders: { [env]: path } })` before `createChat` when saving, else `Chat.setWorkdir` on every picked member whose environment is that one (as "Start task" does for one agent) unless the project's folder there is already this path. **Create project** goes to `/projects/new?name=<folder name>&env=&path=&origin=` (`newProjectLink`), where `NewProject` reads the query (`projectPrefillOf`) into `ProjectForm.initial`: the name and one folder row whose badge carries the origin, so Find on the other rows and `detect` work at once. Whatever the dialog does, the `/chats/new` URL is replaced (the new chat, the form, or `/chats` on cancel), so the link never re-fires.
    - **"Start task":** on Tasks (topbar) and an agent's page. It opens a chat with the agent titled by the objective, sets the folder there and posts the objective, so the task has a user origin to follow up in and core needed no new origin kind.
    - **Agent Config tab:** the "Default working folder" fills `AgentForm`'s new `workdir` slot. It sets `execution.defaultEnvironmentId` + `defaultWorkdir`, and another environment clears the folder. **Account (#414):** an "Account" select on a daemon runtime (`AgentForm.accounts`, `AccountOption { value: AccountKey, runtime }` from `accountOptions(directory.accounts())` — "<label> (<identity>) — on pc, mac", filtered to the draft's runtime) binds `execution.account` (the draft holds the key, `accountOf` parses it back; a key of another runtime binds nothing); picking an account drops the pin and its folder, pinning ("Pin to one environment (advanced)") drops the account; a key no environment reports any more is said under the field. `configPatch` sends `account: null` / `defaultEnvironmentId: null` for what the form dropped, since a patch merges `execution` one level deep.
    - **New schedule:** "Machine" (#414, `ScheduleSpec.machineId`: exclusive with Environment and the folder, compatible with Project; picking an environment drops it); "Working folder" beside Environment, as `ScheduleSpec.workdir`; "Project" (#333) as a third, exclusive option — choosing one clears and disables Environment and Working folder, and `newScheduleSpec` emits `projectId` alone.
  - **Session page:** "Working dir" is the record's `spec.cwd`.
  - **History:** files `workdir.worktree-created` under "Environments and folders".

- Daemon updates in the web app (#367; EXE-06/08/09, OPS-03/04, AGT-09; page-local, nothing in `@agentic/ui`):
  - **Update card** (`pages/machines/{update,UpdateCard,UpdatePolicyForm,LiveUpdateCard,MockUpdateCard}`), under the Machine page's header: `Machine.updateState()` read live. It shows "Running `<version>` (`<channel>`, built `<commit>`)" and, when a release is `available`, its version, "What's new" (`notesUrl`) and three actions: **Update when idle** (`requestUpdate({ mode: 'drain' })`), **Update now** (a confirm whose text is `impactText(impact)`, naming each running turn as a link, then `mode: 'now'`) and **Schedule…** (the policy form switches to a window). A pending update lists its phases (`phaseSteps`), with the download's progress and, while draining, "waiting for N running turns" with links, plus **Cancel** (`cancelUpdate`). Then `last` reads as "Updated to … at …" or the failure ("the previous version was restored" when rolled back), and **Roll back to `<from>`** (`target: 'previous'`) after an applied update. A daemon without a build or the `update` feature — or a 409 "reinstall once" — gets the installer line without a code (`reinstallCommand`: upgrades in place, stays paired). `outdated` is an error banner. `restarts` ≥ 3 with `lastExit` inside the hour is a warning line (`restartWarning`; the view carries the count, not the log). A request is followed like `envResult`: its id, the live read settles it (a phase, or `last.requestId`), a `CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS` timer says when the daemon is slow — no polling.
  - **Channel and policy:** `UpdatePolicyForm` edits a channel and a policy (`manual`, `auto-when-idle`, `window` = the Schedules page's cron field in the workspace zone + hours). On a machine, "Workspace default (…)" is `null` → `setChannel` / `setUpdatePolicy`. In Settings → "Machine updates" (`UpdateDefaults`) it is `Workspace.updateSettings({ updates })`, saved apart from the settings form.
  - **Machines list:** each group reads `updateState()` live for a pill (`updateBadge`: update required / updating / draining / update available) and hands it up; **Update all machines** (`UpdateAll`) confirms by name and asks every online machine that can update itself with a release waiting (`updatable`) for a `drain` update, listing each answer. The caption everywhere is `buildLabel` (`daemon 0.2.0 · stable · abc1234`, else `daemon <daemonVersion>`).
  - **History and Inbox:** the five `machine.update-*` / `channel-set` / `update-policy-set` kinds file under "Machines", labelled and linked to `/machines/:id`. The machine notices (`update-available`, `update-applied`, `update-failed`, `daemon-crash-loop`, `harness-update-available`) are `machine` rows in "Needs you" (`MachineNotice`): the machine's link and Dismiss (`Inbox.ack`).
  - **Mock:** `mock/ops.ts` `opsUpdateStates` holds every state (available, each phase, applied, rolled back, failed, outdated, crash loop, no feature), with a "Preview" picker on the mock Machine page.
- Runtimes in the web app (#370; EXE-08, AGT-09, OPS-03, PLG-02, PLG-09; page-local, nothing in `@agentic/ui`):
  - **"Runtimes on this machine"** (`pages/machines/{harness,HarnessCard,LiveHarnessCard,MockHarnessCard}`, `#runtimes`), under the environments: the rows (`harnessRows`) come from the live `Machine.get()` — each reported runtime's status (ready / not installed / broken), installed version, the version the release ships (`versionLine`), the environments on it. **Install** goes out at once; **Update…** confirms with `harnessImpactText(row, mode)` — its running turns (links) are interrupted and offered Resume (`now`) or waited for (`drain`, the "When" choice), its live sessions restart, other runtimes keep running; **Remove…** is off with the reason while an environment runs on it (`removeBlocked`), else confirms. The request is followed like `envResult` (live `harnessResult`, a `CLIENT_TIMEOUT_MS` timer); one asked elsewhere shows from `get().harnessRequest`. Phases (`harnessSteps`), then the outcome line (`outcomeLine`, the daemon's code in words), or the refusal on its row. A daemon without `features: ['harness']` gets the reinstall line.
  - **Machines list:** a second pill counts the runtimes with a newer harness (`harnessUpdates`). **Runtime plugin page:** a harness runtime's `/plugins/<runtime>` has a "Machines" section (`plugins/RuntimeMachines`): each paired machine has it (version, and a newer one waiting), lacks it, has it broken, or predates harnesses, linking to `/machines/<id>#runtimes`.
  - **History and Inbox:** `harness.changed` files under "Machines" as `harness installed` / `updated` / `removed` / `failed`, linked to the runtimes card; the `harness-update-available` notice reads RUNTIME UPDATE and opens the runtimes card.
  - **Mock:** `opsHarnessStates` (update available, requested, downloading, draining, installing, updated, failed, broken, no feature) with a "Preview" picker; the mock Plugin page lists the sample machines.

#### #725 web: projects scaffold — routes, sub-menu seam, stubs and slots so page issues never collide

Every shared file the redesign needs, edited once, so a page issue only adds files in its own folder or replaces its own slot line (the #649 precedent).

- **Routes** (`router.ts`, literals before `:id`): `/projects` (`projects`, `index/ProjectsIndex` — today's list until #729), `/projects/links` (`projects-links`), `/projects/new` (`project-new`, today's form), and inside a project `/projects/:id` (`project`, Overview), `…/chats`, `…/work`, `…/work/:item` (`pr:<n>` is the pull request page, anything else the item page — `work/WorkItemRoute`), `…/requests`, `…/plan` (`?view=list|board|graph`, `features/plan/Plan`), `…/settings/:tab` (general, members, folders, connectors, features, manager — `settings/SettingsLayout` and `settings/tabs.ts`) and `…/f/:feature` (a feature's `Section` from `features/registry.ts`). Route names `project-*`; `CRUMBS` roots them all at Projects.
- **Layout seam** (`pages/projects/layout/`): the route table registers `inProject(Page)`, so every page under `/projects/:id` gets `{ project: ProjectRecord }` from `ProjectLayout` (the sample workspace on mock data, `Workspace.projects()` live; not found / signed out otherwise) and never loads it. Live, the layout publishes `projectHead` (`{ id, name, features, manager }`).
- **Sub-menu seam**: `NavItem.children?: readonly NavGroup[]` in `@agentic/ui`'s shell, drawn minimally (indented blocks, headed after the first; the page is the longest matching sub-item). `App.tsx` passes `projectMenuFor(route)` (`layout/menu.ts`) as `NAV_GROUPS(badge, project)`: Core (Overview, Chats, Work, Requests when the project has a coordinator), Features (enabled features whose registry entry has a `Section`), Settings (the six tabs).
- **Crumbs**: each page's topbar returns `trail: projectTrail(route, …)` — always `Projects › <project> › …`; Overview keeps route `project`'s crumb. `TRAILS['projects-links']` is Projects › Links.
- **Feature registry** (`pages/projects/features/registry.ts`): feature id → `{ label?, path?, Section?, OverviewCard? }`, one slot line each for git (#746) and plan (#754).
- **Stubs**: one component per page folder (`index/`, `links/`, `overview/`, `chats/`, `work/`, `work/item/`, `work/pull/`, `requests/`, `settings/{general,members,folders,connectors,features,manager}/`, `features/plan/{list,board,graph}/`, `features/git/`), each rendering its title and "Coming in #N". Settings › General is today's project form until #733.
- **CSS / mocks / tests**: `styles/projects/index.css` (one `@import` from `pages.css`) over one file per page; `mock/projects/<area>.ts` per page, imported by nothing shared; `pages/projects*.test.tsx` split into `__tests__/projects/{index,settings,live,model}.test.ts(x)`, with the scaffold's own `routes.test.tsx` / `routes-live.test.tsx`.
- **`@agentic/ui` projects barrel** (`packages/ui/src/projects/`): `ProjectSquare`, `StageTrack`, `ItemGlyph`, `SlotMarks` (+ `usedSlots`), `ChecksBar`, `PullCard` with their final props and placeholder renders.
- **Slot lines** (`// slot #N <what> — replace this line`) for the Pulls (#742), Plan (#750) and Requests (#758) actors (platform index; web `actors/{defs,client,keys}.ts`, `actors.app.ts`; audit kinds, data shapes and by-kind map), the `plan` (#751) and `requests` (#759) tool families (runtimes `tools/index.ts` and `tools/ports.ts`; mcp `server/tools.ts` and `server/port.ts`), and the plan project feature (#753) in `plugins/features.ts`.

#### #726 ui: ProjectSquare, StageTrack, ItemGlyph, SlotMarks, ChecksBar

The five shared parts in `packages/ui/src/projects/`, drawn to the handoff with inline token styles (no stylesheet to import; happy-dom drops `color-mix()` fills, so tests check the border/text tokens). Props are #725's; colour is never the only signal.

- **`ProjectSquare`**: 22 px default (44 px header, radius 10 from 36 px), the four agent hues by `ProjectColor` (`--ag-agent-1…4`, fill 12 %, border 40 %); unset, `projectColorFor(id ?? name)` hashes onto `PROJECT_COLORS`. Decorative (`aria-hidden`) — the name sits beside it.
- **`StageTrack`**: 132 px, 6 px segments, 3 px gap; passed `--color-primary`, current by `WorkStageState` (`info` / `warning` / `error` / `primary` when done), later `--ag-line-strong`; name mono 11 below unless `bare`. `role="img"` named `Checks, stage 4 of 6, failed`; `stage` is clamped.
- **`ItemGlyph`**: 16 px, the six plan states per the Plan table. Named by its state (`Needs you`) by default; `label=""` makes it decorative for rows that say the state in text.
- **`SlotMarks`**: a `<ul aria-label="Feature slots">` of five 22 px marks (Section, Overview card, Work stages, Chat context, Agent instructions and tools), each `<slot>: used|not used` as name and tooltip; `data-used` on the list keeps `usedSlots` order.
- **`ChecksBar`**: 96 × 6 px on `line-strong`, `flex-grow` per count (passed, running + queued, failed); summary `N failing · N running · N queued`, else `P/N passed` (skipped left out), else `no checks`.
